1→"""Python extraction: function bodies, class structure, param patterns."""
     2→
     3→import hashlib
     4→import re
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→from desloppify.engine.detectors.base import FunctionInfo
     8→from desloppify.engine.detectors.passthrough import (
     9→    classify_params,
    10→    classify_passthrough_tier,
    11→)
    12→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import find_py_files
    13→from desloppify.languages.python.extractors_classes import extract_py_classes
    14→from desloppify.languages.python.extractors_shared import (
    15→    extract_py_params,
    16→    find_block_end,
    17→    read_file,
    18→)
    19→
    20→
    21→def _find_signature_end(lines: list[str], start: int) -> int | None:
    22→    """Find the line where a function signature closes."""
    23→    for j in range(start, len(lines)):
    24→        lt = lines[j]
    25→        if ")" in lt and ":" in lt[lt.rindex(")") + 1 :]:
    26→            return j
    27→        if j > start and lt.strip().endswith(":"):
    28→            return j
    29→    return None
    30→
    31→
    32→def extract_py_functions(filepath: str) -> list[FunctionInfo]:
    33→    """Extract function bodies from a Python file using indentation-based boundaries."""
    34→    content = read_file(filepath)
    35→    if content is None:
    36→        return []
    37→    lines = content.splitlines()
    38→    functions = []
    39→    fn_re = re.compile(r"^(\s*)(?:async\s+)?def\s+(\w+)\s*\(")
    40→    i = 0
    41→    while i < len(lines):
    42→        m = fn_re.match(lines[i])
    43→        if not m:
    44→            i += 1
    45→            continue
    46→        fn_indent = len(m.group(1))
    47→        name = m.group(2)
    48→        start_line = i
    49→        j = _find_signature_end(lines, i)
    50→        if j is None:
    51→            i += 1
    52→            continue
    53→
    54→        # Extract params from multi-line signature
    55→        sig_text = "\n".join(lines[start_line : j + 1])
    56→        if "(" not in sig_text or ")" not in sig_text:
    57→            i += 1
    58→            continue
    59→        open_paren = sig_text.index("(")
    60→        close_paren = sig_text.rindex(")")
    61→        param_str = sig_text[open_paren + 1 : close_paren]
    62→        params = extract_py_params(param_str)
    63→
    64→        # Find body extent: all lines indented past fn_indent, trim trailing blanks
    65→        block_end = find_block_end(lines, j + 1, fn_indent)
    66→        end_line = block_end
    67→        while end_line > j + 1 and not lines[end_line - 1].strip():
    68→            end_line -= 1
    69→        body = "\n".join(lines[start_line:end_line])
    70→        normalized = normalize_py_body(body)
    71→        if len(normalized.splitlines()) >= 3:
    72→            functions.append(
    73→                FunctionInfo(
    74→                    name=name,
    75→                    file=filepath,
    76→                    line=start_line + 1,
    77→                    end_line=end_line,
    78→                    loc=end_line - start_line,
    79→                    body=body,
    80→                    normalized=normalized,
    81→                    body_hash=hashlib.md5(normalized.encode()).hexdigest(),
    82→                    params=params,
    83→                )
    84→            )
    85→        i = end_line
    86→    return functions
    87→
    88→
    89→def normalize_py_body(body: str) -> str:
    90→    """Normalize a Python function body: strip docstrings, comments, print/logging."""
    91→    lines = body.splitlines()
    92→    normalized = []
    93→    in_docstring = False
    94→    docstring_quote = None
    95→    for line in lines:
    96→        stripped = line.strip()
    97→        if in_docstring:
    98→            if docstring_quote and docstring_quote in stripped:
    99→                in_docstring = False
   100→            continue
   101→        if stripped.startswith('"""') or stripped.startswith("'''"):
   102→            docstring_quote = stripped[:3]
   103→            if stripped.count(docstring_quote) >= 2:
   104→                continue
   105→            in_docstring = True
   106→            continue
   107→        if not stripped or stripped.startswith("#"):
   108→            continue
   109→        cp = stripped.find("  #")
   110→        if cp > 0:
   111→            stripped = stripped[:cp].rstrip()
   112→        if re.match(r"(?:print\s*\(|(?:logging|logger|log)\.\w+\s*\()", stripped):
   113→            continue
   114→        if stripped:
   115→            normalized.append(stripped)
   116→    return "\n".join(normalized)
   117→
   118→
   119→def py_passthrough_pattern(name: str) -> str:
   120→    """Match same-name keyword arg: param=param in a function call."""
   121→    escaped = re.escape(name)
   122→    return rf"\b{escaped}\s*=\s*{escaped}\b"
   123→
   124→
   125→_PY_DEF_RE = re.compile(r"^def\s+(\w+)\s*\(", re.MULTILINE)
   126→
   127→
   128→def detect_passthrough_functions(path: Path) -> list[dict]:
   129→    """Detect Python functions where most params are same-name forwarded."""
   130→    entries = []
   131→    for filepath in find_py_files(path):
   132→        content = read_file(filepath)
   133→        if content is None:
   134→            continue
   135→        for m in _PY_DEF_RE.finditer(content):
   136→            name = m.group(1)
   137→            depth = 1
   138→            i = m.end()
   139→            while i < len(content) and depth > 0:
   140→                ch = content[i]
   141→                if ch == "(":
   142→                    depth += 1
   143→                elif ch == ")":
   144→                    depth -= 1
   145→                i += 1
   146→            if depth != 0:
   147→                continue
   148→            param_str = content[m.end() : i - 1]
   149→            params = extract_py_params(param_str)
   150→            if len(params) < 4:
   151→                continue
   152→            rest_after_paren = content[i:]
   153→            colon_m = re.search(r":", rest_after_paren)
   154→            if not colon_m:
   155→                continue
   156→            rest = rest_after_paren[colon_m.end() :]
   157→            bm = re.search(r"\n(?=[^\s\n#])", rest)
   158→            body = rest[: bm.start()] if bm else rest
   159→
   160→            has_kwargs_spread = bool(re.search(r"\*\*kwargs\b", body))
   161→            pt, direct = classify_params(
   162→                params, body, py_passthrough_pattern, occurrences_per_match=2
   163→            )
   164→
   165→            if len(pt) < 4 and not has_kwargs_spread:
   166→                continue
   167→            ratio = len(pt) / len(params)
   168→            classification = classify_passthrough_tier(
   169→                len(pt), ratio, has_spread=has_kwargs_spread
   170→            )
   171→            if classification is None:
   172→                continue
   173→            tier, confidence = classification
   174→
   175→            entries.append(
   176→                {
   177→                    "file": filepath,
   178→                    "function": name,
   179→                    "total_params": len(params),
   180→                    "passthrough": len(pt),
   181→                    "direct": len(direct),
   182→                    "ratio": round(ratio, 2),
   183→                    "line": content[: m.start()].count("\n") + 1,
   184→                    "tier": tier,
   185→                    "confidence": confidence,
   186→                    "passthrough_params": sorted(pt),
   187→                    "direct_params": sorted(direct),
   188→                    "has_kwargs_spread": has_kwargs_spread,
   189→                }
   190→            )
   191→
   192→    return sorted(entries, key=lambda e: (-e["passthrough"], -e["ratio"]))
   193→
   194→
   195→__all__ = [
   196→    "detect_passthrough_functions",
   197→    "extract_py_classes",
   198→    "extract_py_functions",
   199→    "extract_py_params",
   200→    "normalize_py_body",
   201→    "py_passthrough_pattern",
   202→]
   203→
```

1→"""Quality and maintainability oriented tree-level smell detectors."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import ast
     6→
     7→from desloppify.languages.python.detectors.smells_ast._shared import (
     8→    _is_docstring,
     9→    _is_log_or_print,
    10→    _is_return_none,
    11→    _is_trivial_if,
    12→    _iter_nodes,
    13→)
    14→from desloppify.languages.python.detectors.smells_ast._tree_quality_detectors_types import (
    15→    _detect_annotation_quality as _detect_annotation_quality,
    16→)
    17→from desloppify.languages.python.detectors.smells_ast._tree_quality_detectors_types import (
    18→    _detect_optional_param_sprawl as _detect_optional_param_sprawl,
    19→)
    20→
    21→__all__ = [
    22→    "_detect_annotation_quality",
    23→    "_detect_constant_return",
    24→    "_detect_noop_function",
    25→    "_detect_optional_param_sprawl",
    26→    "_detect_unreachable_code",
    27→]
    28→
    29→
    30→def _detect_unreachable_code(
    31→    filepath: str,
    32→    tree: ast.Module,
    33→    *,
    34→    all_nodes: tuple[ast.AST, ...] | None = None,
    35→) -> list[dict]:
    36→    """Flag statements after unconditional return/raise/break/continue.
    37→
    38→    Walks every statement block (function body, if/else body, etc.) and flags
    39→    any statement that follows an unconditional flow-control statement.
    40→    """
    41→    _TERMINAL = (ast.Return, ast.Raise, ast.Break, ast.Continue)
    42→    results: list[dict] = []
    43→
    44→    def _check_block(stmts: list[ast.stmt]):
    45→        for i, stmt in enumerate(stmts):
    46→            if isinstance(stmt, _TERMINAL) and i < len(stmts) - 1:
    47→                next_stmt = stmts[i + 1]
    48→                # Skip flagging string constants (often used as section markers)
    49→                if isinstance(next_stmt, ast.Expr) and isinstance(
    50→                    next_stmt.value, ast.Constant
    51→                ):
    52→                    continue
    53→                results.append(
    54→                    {
    55→                        "file": filepath,
    56→                        "line": next_stmt.lineno,
    57→                        "content": f"unreachable after {type(stmt).__name__.lower()} on line {stmt.lineno}",
    58→                    }
    59→                )
    60→            # Recurse into compound statements
    61→            for attr in ("body", "orelse", "finalbody", "handlers"):
    62→                block = getattr(stmt, attr, None)
    63→                if isinstance(block, list):
    64→                    child_stmts = [s for s in block if isinstance(s, ast.stmt)]
    65→                    if child_stmts:
    66→                        _check_block(child_stmts)
    67→            # ExceptHandler has a body too
    68→            if isinstance(stmt, ast.ExceptHandler):
    69→                _check_block(stmt.body)
    70→
    71→    for node in _iter_nodes(tree, all_nodes, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):
    72→        _check_block(node.body)
    73→    return results
    74→
    75→
    76→def _detect_constant_return(
    77→    filepath: str,
    78→    tree: ast.Module,
    79→    *,
    80→    all_nodes: tuple[ast.AST, ...] | None = None,
    81→) -> list[dict]:
    82→    """Flag functions that always return the same constant value.
    83→
    84→    Analyzes all return paths — if every return statement returns the same
    85→    literal value (True, False, None, a number, or a string), the function
    86→    likely has dead logic or is a stub masquerading as real code.
    87→    """
    88→    results: list[dict] = []
    89→    for node in _iter_nodes(tree, all_nodes, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):
    90→        # Skip tiny functions (stubs/pass-only already caught by dead_function)
    91→        if not hasattr(node, "end_lineno") or not node.end_lineno:
    92→            continue
    93→        loc = node.end_lineno - node.lineno + 1
    94→        if loc < 4:
    95→            continue
    96→        # Skip decorated functions (properties, abstractmethods, etc.)
    97→        if node.decorator_list:
    98→            continue
    99→
   100→        returns = []
   101→        has_conditional = False
   102→        for child in ast.walk(node):
   103→            if isinstance(child, ast.Return):
   104→                returns.append(child)
   105→            if isinstance(
   106→                child,
   107→                ast.If | ast.For | ast.While | ast.With | ast.Try | ast.ExceptHandler,
   108→            ):
   109→                has_conditional = True
   110→
   111→        # Need at least 2 returns and some conditional logic to be interesting
   112→        if len(returns) < 2 or not has_conditional:
   113→            continue
   114→
   115→        # Extract constant values from all returns
   116→        values = set()
   117→        all_constant = True
   118→        for ret in returns:
   119→            if ret.value is None:
   120→                values.add(repr(None))
   121→            elif isinstance(ret.value, ast.Constant):
   122→                values.add(repr(ret.value.value))
   123→            else:
   124→                all_constant = False
   125→                break
   126→
   127→        if all_constant and len(values) == 1:
   128→            val = next(iter(values))
   129→            # Skip functions that always return None — they're just procedures
   130→            if val == "None":
   131→                continue
   132→            results.append(
   133→                {
   134→                    "file": filepath,
   135→                    "line": node.lineno,
   136→                    "content": f"{node.name}() always returns {val} ({len(returns)} return sites)",
   137→                }
   138→            )
   139→    return results
   140→
   141→
   142→def _detect_noop_function(
   143→    filepath: str,
   144→    tree: ast.Module,
   145→    *,
   146→    all_nodes: tuple[ast.AST, ...] | None = None,
   147→) -> list[dict]:
   148→    """Flag non-trivial functions whose body does nothing useful.
   149→
   150→    A function is noop if its body contains only: pass, return, logging calls,
   151→    and early-return ifs with trivial bodies. Excludes __init__, abstract methods,
   152→    property getters, short functions (< 3 statements), and decorated functions.
   153→    """
   154→    _SKIP_NAMES = {
   155→        "__init__",
   156→        "__str__",
   157→        "__repr__",
   158→        "__enter__",
   159→        "__exit__",
   160→        "__del__",
   161→        "__hash__",
   162→        "__eq__",
   163→        "__lt__",
   164→        "__le__",
   165→        "__gt__",
   166→        "__ge__",
   167→        "__ne__",
   168→        "__bool__",
   169→        "__len__",
   170→    }
   171→
   172→    results: list[dict] = []
   173→    for node in _iter_nodes(tree, all_nodes, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):
   174→        if node.name in _SKIP_NAMES:
   175→            continue
   176→        # Skip decorated functions (abstract methods, properties, etc.)
   177→        if node.decorator_list:
   178→            continue
   179→        # Skip short functions — dead_function already catches 1-2 statement bodies
   180→        body = node.body
   181→        # Strip leading docstring
   182→        if body and _is_docstring(body[0]):
   183→            body = body[1:]
   184→        if len(body) < 3:
   185→            continue
   186→
   187→        # Check if every statement is trivial
   188→        all_trivial = True
   189→        for stmt in body:
   190→            if isinstance(stmt, ast.Pass):
   191→                continue
   192→            if _is_return_none(stmt):
   193→                continue
   194→            if _is_log_or_print(stmt):
   195→                continue
   196→            if _is_trivial_if(stmt):
   197→                continue
   198→            all_trivial = False
   199→            break
   200→
   201→        if all_trivial:
   202→            results.append(
   203→                {
   204→                    "file": filepath,
   205→                    "line": node.lineno,
   206→                    "content": f"{node.name}() — {len(body)} statements, all trivial (pass/return/log)",
   207→                }
   208→            )
   209→    return results
   210→

1→"""AST detectors that operate on function/class nodes."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import ast
     6→
     7→from desloppify.languages.python.detectors.smells_ast._shared import (
     8→    _is_docstring,
     9→    _is_return_none,
    10→)
    11→
    12→
    13→def _is_test_file(filepath: str) -> bool:
    14→    """Return True when a path clearly points to a test module."""
    15→    normalized = filepath.replace("\\", "/")
    16→    return normalized.startswith("tests/") or "/tests/" in normalized
    17→
    18→
    19→def _detect_monster_functions(filepath: str, node: ast.AST) -> list[dict]:
    20→    """Flag functions longer than 150 LOC."""
    21→    if not (hasattr(node, "end_lineno") and node.end_lineno):
    22→        return []
    23→    loc = node.end_lineno - node.lineno + 1
    24→    if loc > 150:
    25→        return [
    26→            {
    27→                "file": filepath,
    28→                "line": node.lineno,
    29→                "content": f"{node.name}() — {loc} LOC",
    30→            }
    31→        ]
    32→    return []
    33→
    34→
    35→def _detect_dead_functions(filepath: str, node: ast.AST) -> list[dict]:
    36→    """Flag functions whose body is only pass, return, or return None."""
    37→    if node.decorator_list:
    38→        return []
    39→    body = node.body
    40→    if len(body) == 1:
    41→        stmt = body[0]
    42→        if isinstance(stmt, ast.Pass) or _is_return_none(stmt):
    43→            return [
    44→                {
    45→                    "file": filepath,
    46→                    "line": node.lineno,
    47→                    "content": f"{node.name}() — body is only {ast.dump(stmt)[:40]}",
    48→                }
    49→            ]
    50→    elif len(body) == 2:
    51→        first, second = body
    52→        if not _is_docstring(first):
    53→            return []
    54→        if isinstance(second, ast.Pass):
    55→            desc = "docstring + pass"
    56→        elif _is_return_none(second):
    57→            desc = "docstring + return None"
    58→        else:
    59→            return []
    60→        return [
    61→            {
    62→                "file": filepath,
    63→                "line": node.lineno,
    64→                "content": f"{node.name}() — {desc}",
    65→            }
    66→        ]
    67→    return []
    68→
    69→
    70→def _detect_deferred_imports(filepath: str, node: ast.AST) -> list[dict]:
    71→    """Flag function-level imports (possible circular import workarounds)."""
    72→    if _is_test_file(filepath):
    73→        return []
    74→    _SKIP_MODULES = ("typing", "typing_extensions", "__future__")
    75→    for child in ast.walk(node):
    76→        if (
    77→            not isinstance(child, ast.Import | ast.ImportFrom)
    78→            or child.lineno <= node.lineno
    79→        ):
    80→            continue
    81→        module = getattr(child, "module", None) or ""
    82→        if module in _SKIP_MODULES:
    83→            continue
    84→        names = ", ".join(a.name for a in child.names[:3])
    85→        if len(child.names) > 3:
    86→            names += f", +{len(child.names) - 3}"
    87→        return [
    88→            {
    89→                "file": filepath,
    90→                "line": child.lineno,
    91→                "content": f"import {module or names} inside {node.name}()",
    92→            }
    93→        ]
    94→    return []
    95→
    96→
    97→def _detect_inline_classes(filepath: str, node: ast.AST) -> list[dict]:
    98→    """Flag classes defined inside functions."""
    99→    results: list[dict] = []
   100→    for child in node.body:
   101→        if isinstance(child, ast.ClassDef):
   102→            results.append(
   103→                {
   104→                    "file": filepath,
   105→                    "line": child.lineno,
   106→                    "content": f"class {child.name} defined inside {node.name}()",
   107→                }
   108→            )
   109→    return results
   110→
   111→
   112→_FUNC_TYPES = (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef, ast.Lambda)
   113→_BLOCK_ATTRS = ("body", "handlers", "orelse", "finalbody")
   114→
   115→
   116→def _walk_inner_defs(
   117→    body: list[ast.AST], depth: int, inner_defs: list[ast.AST],
   118→) -> int:
   119→    """Recursively collect inner function/lambda defs and return max nesting depth."""
   120→    max_depth = 0
   121→    for child in body:
   122→        if isinstance(child, _FUNC_TYPES):
   123→            inner_defs.append(child)
   124→            current_depth = depth + 1
   125→            max_depth = max(max_depth, current_depth)
   126→            child_body = getattr(child, "body", None)
   127→            if isinstance(child_body, list):
   128→                max_depth = max(
   129→                    max_depth,
   130→                    _walk_inner_defs(child_body, current_depth, inner_defs),
   131→                )
   132→            elif isinstance(child, ast.Lambda):
   133→                max_depth = max(
   134→                    max_depth,
   135→                    _collect_nested_lambdas(child, current_depth, inner_defs),
   136→                )
   137→        else:
   138→            for attr in _BLOCK_ATTRS:
   139→                sub_body = getattr(child, attr, None)
   140→                if isinstance(sub_body, list):
   141→                    max_depth = max(
   142→                        max_depth, _walk_inner_defs(sub_body, depth, inner_defs),
   143→                    )
   144→    return max_depth
   145→
   146→
   147→def _collect_nested_lambdas(
   148→    parent: ast.Lambda, depth: int, inner_defs: list[ast.AST],
   149→) -> int:
   150→    """Walk a lambda's expression body for nested lambdas."""
   151→    max_depth = depth
   152→    for sub in ast.walk(parent.body):
   153→        if isinstance(sub, ast.Lambda) and sub is not parent:
   154→            inner_defs.append(sub)
   155→            max_depth = max(max_depth, depth + 1)
   156→    return max_depth
   157→
   158→
   159→def _format_inner_def_names(inner_defs: list[ast.AST]) -> str:
   160→    """Format inner def names for the finding content string."""
   161→    names = [
   162→        getattr(d, "name", "<lambda>")
   163→        for d in inner_defs[:5]
   164→        if isinstance(d, ast.FunctionDef | ast.AsyncFunctionDef)
   165→    ]
   166→    names_str = ", ".join(names) if names else "<lambdas>"
   167→    if len(inner_defs) > 5:
   168→        names_str += ", ..."
   169→    return names_str
   170→
   171→
   172→def _detect_nested_closures(filepath: str, node: ast.AST) -> list[dict]:
   173→    """Flag functions with deeply nested inner defs or too many inner defs total."""
   174→    inner_defs: list[ast.AST] = []
   175→    max_depth = _walk_inner_defs(node.body, 0, inner_defs)
   176→
   177→    if max_depth < 2 and len(inner_defs) < 3:
   178→        return []
   179→    return [
   180→        {
   181→            "file": filepath,
   182→            "line": node.lineno,
   183→            "content": (
   184→                f"{node.name}() — {len(inner_defs)} inner defs"
   185→                f" (depth {max_depth}): {_format_inner_def_names(inner_defs)}"
   186→            ),
   187→        }
   188→    ]
   189→
   190→
   191→def _collect_single_list_assignments(body: list[ast.AST]) -> dict[str, int]:
   192→    """Return {name: lineno} for `x = [single_value]` assignments."""
   193→    result: dict[str, int] = {}
   194→    for stmt in body:
   195→        if not isinstance(stmt, ast.Assign) or len(stmt.targets) != 1:
   196→            continue
   197→        target = stmt.targets[0]
   198→        if (
   199→            isinstance(target, ast.Name)
   200→            and isinstance(stmt.value, ast.List)
   201→            and len(stmt.value.elts) == 1
   202→        ):
   203→            result[target.id] = stmt.lineno
   204→    return result
   205→
   206→
   207→def _find_subscript_zero_refs(
   208→    node: ast.AST, candidate_names: set[str],
   209→) -> set[str]:
   210→    """Find names from candidate_names accessed as x[0] inside nested functions."""
   211→    used: set[str] = set()
   212→    for child in ast.walk(node):
   213→        if child is node or not isinstance(child, _FUNC_TYPES):
   214→            continue
   215→        for sub in ast.walk(child):
   216→            if not isinstance(sub, ast.Subscript):
   217→                continue
   218→            if not isinstance(sub.value, ast.Name):
   219→                continue
   220→            if sub.value.id not in candidate_names:
   221→                continue
   222→            if isinstance(sub.slice, ast.Constant) and sub.slice.value == 0:
   223→                used.add(sub.value.id)
   224→    return used
   225→
   226→
   227→def _detect_mutable_ref_hack(filepath: str, node: ast.AST) -> list[dict]:
   228→    """Flag mutable-list-as-ref hacks: x = [value] then x[0] inside a nested function."""
   229→    single_list_names = _collect_single_list_assignments(node.body)
   230→    if not single_list_names:
   231→        return []
   232→
   233→    used_names = _find_subscript_zero_refs(node, set(single_list_names))
   234→    return [
   235→        {
   236→            "file": filepath,
   237→            "line": single_list_names[name],
   238→            "content": (
   239→                f"{name} = [v] in {node.name}()"
   240→                " — mutable-list ref hack (use nonlocal or a dataclass)"
   241→            ),
   242→        }
   243→        for name in sorted(used_names)
   244→    ]
   245→
   246→
   247→# Node types that contribute to cyclomatic complexity.
   248→_DECISION_TYPES = (
   249→    ast.If, ast.IfExp, ast.For, ast.AsyncFor, ast.While,
   250→    ast.ExceptHandler, ast.With, ast.AsyncWith, ast.Assert,
   251→)
   252→
   253→
   254→def _compute_cyclomatic_complexity(node: ast.AST) -> int:
   255→    """Compute cyclomatic complexity for a function AST node."""
   256→    complexity = 1
   257→    for child in ast.walk(node):
   258→        if isinstance(child, _DECISION_TYPES):
   259→            complexity += 1
   260→        elif isinstance(child, ast.BoolOp):
   261→            complexity += len(child.values) - 1
   262→    return complexity
   263→
   264→
   265→def _detect_high_cyclomatic_complexity(filepath: str, node: ast.AST) -> list[dict]:
   266→    """Flag functions with cyclomatic complexity > 12."""
   267→    complexity = _compute_cyclomatic_complexity(node)
   268→    if complexity > 12:
   269→        return [
   270→            {
   271→                "file": filepath,
   272→                "line": node.lineno,
   273→                "content": f"{node.name}() — cyclomatic complexity {complexity}",
   274→            }
   275→        ]
   276→    return []
   277→
   278→
   279→def _detect_lru_cache_mutable(
   280→    filepath: str,
   281→    node: ast.AST,
   282→    tree: ast.Module,
   283→) -> list[dict]:
   284→    """Flag @lru_cache/@cache functions that reference module-level mutable variables.
   285→
   286→    Finds globals referenced in the function body that aren't in the parameter list,
   287→    checking if those names are assigned to mutable values at module level.
   288→    """
   289→    # Check if this function has @lru_cache or @cache decorator
   290→    has_cache = False
   291→    for dec in node.decorator_list:
   292→        if isinstance(dec, ast.Name) and dec.id in ("lru_cache", "cache"):
   293→            has_cache = True
   294→        elif isinstance(dec, ast.Call) and isinstance(dec.func, ast.Name):
   295→            if dec.func.id in ("lru_cache", "cache"):
   296→                has_cache = True
   297→        elif isinstance(dec, ast.Attribute) and dec.attr in ("lru_cache", "cache"):
   298→            has_cache = True
   299→    if not has_cache:
   300→        return []
   301→
   302→    # Get parameter names
   303→    param_names = set()
   304→    for arg in node.args.args + node.args.posonlyargs + node.args.kwonlyargs:
   305→        param_names.add(arg.arg)
   306→    if node.args.vararg:
   307→        param_names.add(node.args.vararg.arg)
   308→    if node.args.kwarg:
   309→        param_names.add(node.args.kwarg.arg)
   310→
   311→    # Collect module-level mutable assignments
   312→    module_mutables = set()
   313→    for stmt in tree.body:
   314→        if isinstance(stmt, ast.Assign):
   315→            for target in stmt.targets:
   316→                if isinstance(target, ast.Name) and isinstance(
   317→                    stmt.value, ast.List | ast.Dict | ast.Set | ast.Call
   318→                ):
   319→                    module_mutables.add(target.id)
   320→        elif (
   321→            isinstance(stmt, ast.AnnAssign)
   322→            and stmt.target
   323→            and isinstance(stmt.target, ast.Name)
   324→        ):
   325→            if stmt.value and isinstance(
   326→                stmt.value, ast.List | ast.Dict | ast.Set | ast.Call
   327→            ):
   328→                module_mutables.add(stmt.target.id)
   329→
   330→    # Find Name references in function body that point to module-level mutables
   331→    for child in ast.walk(node):
   332→        if (
   333→            isinstance(child, ast.Name)
   334→            and child.id in module_mutables
   335→            and child.id not in param_names
   336→        ):
   337→            return [
   338→                {
   339→                    "file": filepath,
   340→                    "line": node.lineno,
   341→                    "content": f"@lru_cache on {node.name}() reads mutable global '{child.id}'",
   342→                }
   343→            ]
   344→    return []
   345→
```

1→"""Shared helpers and constants for Python AST smell detectors."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import ast
     6→
     7→
     8→def _is_return_none(stmt: ast.AST) -> bool:
     9→    """Check if a statement is `return` or `return None`."""
    10→    if not isinstance(stmt, ast.Return):
    11→        return False
    12→    return stmt.value is None or (
    13→        isinstance(stmt.value, ast.Constant) and stmt.value.value is None
    14→    )
    15→
    16→
    17→def _is_docstring(stmt: ast.AST) -> bool:
    18→    """Check whether a statement is a docstring expression."""
    19→    return isinstance(stmt, ast.Expr) and isinstance(
    20→        stmt.value, ast.Constant | ast.JoinedStr
    21→    )
    22→
    23→
    24→# Variable names that strongly suggest filesystem paths (not module specifiers)
    25→_PATH_VAR_NAMES = {
    26→    "filepath",
    27→    "file_path",
    28→    "filename",
    29→    "file_name",
    30→    "dirpath",
    31→    "dir_path",
    32→    "dirname",
    33→    "dir_name",
    34→    "directory",
    35→    "rel_path",
    36→    "abs_path",
    37→    "rel_file",
    38→    "rel_dir",
    39→    "full_path",
    40→    "base_path",
    41→    "parent_path",
    42→    "scan_path",
    43→}
    44→
    45→# Substrings that suggest a variable holds a path
    46→_PATH_NAME_PARTS = {"filepath", "dirpath", "file_path", "dir_path"}
    47→
    48→
    49→def _looks_like_path_var(name: str) -> bool:
    50→    """Check if a variable name suggests it holds a filesystem path."""
    51→    lower = name.lower()
    52→    if lower in _PATH_VAR_NAMES:
    53→        return True
    54→    # Check for path-related substrings: e.g., old_filepath, scan_path
    55→    return any(part in lower for part in _PATH_NAME_PARTS)
    56→
    57→
    58→def _is_log_or_print(node: ast.AST) -> bool:
    59→    """Check if a statement is a logging/print call."""
    60→    if not isinstance(node, ast.Expr) or not isinstance(node.value, ast.Call):
    61→        return False
    62→    func = node.value.func
    63→    if isinstance(func, ast.Name) and func.id == "print":
    64→        return True
    65→    if isinstance(func, ast.Attribute):
    66→        if isinstance(func.value, ast.Name) and func.value.id in (
    67→            "logger",
    68→            "log",
    69→            "logging",
    70→        ):
    71→            return True
    72→    return False
    73→
    74→
    75→def _is_trivial_if(node: ast.AST) -> bool:
    76→    """Check if an If statement has only trivially-empty body.
    77→
    78→    For noop classification, only `pass` and `return None` should count as
    79→    trivial. Returning concrete values is meaningful logic and should not be
    80→    treated as a no-op.
    81→    """
    82→    if not isinstance(node, ast.If):
    83→        return False
    84→    for stmt in node.body + node.orelse:
    85→        if isinstance(stmt, ast.Pass):
    86→            continue
    87→        if _is_return_none(stmt):
    88→            continue
    89→        if _is_log_or_print(stmt):
    90→            continue
    91→        if isinstance(stmt, ast.If):
    92→            if not _is_trivial_if(stmt):
    93→                return False
    94→            continue
    95→        return False
    96→    return True
    97→
    98→
    99→def _iter_nodes(
   100→    tree: ast.AST,
   101→    all_nodes: tuple[ast.AST, ...] | None,
   102→    node_types,
   103→):
   104→    """Yield nodes of requested types from precomputed or walked AST nodes."""
   105→    if all_nodes is not None:
   106→        return (node for node in all_nodes if isinstance(node, node_types))
   107→    return (node for node in ast.walk(tree) if isinstance(node, node_types))
   108→
```

   330→
   331→_DISPLAY_ORDER = list(DISPLAY_ORDER)
   332→
   333→JUDGMENT_DETECTORS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
   334→    name for name, meta in DETECTORS.items() if meta.needs_judgment
   335→)
   336→_BASE_DETECTORS: dict[str, DetectorMeta] = dict(DETECTORS)
   337→_BASE_DISPLAY_ORDER: list[str] = list(_DISPLAY_ORDER)
   338→_BASE_JUDGMENT_DETECTORS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(JUDGMENT_DETECTORS)
   339→
   340→_on_register_callbacks: list[Callable[[], None]] = []
   341→
   342→
   343→def on_detector_registered(callback: Callable[[], None]) -> None:
   344→    """Register a callback invoked after register_detector(). No-arg."""
   345→    _on_register_callbacks.append(callback)
   346→
   347→
   348→def register_detector(meta: DetectorMeta) -> None:
   349→    """Register a detector at runtime (used by generic plugins)."""
   350→    global JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   351→    DETECTORS[meta.name] = meta
   352→    if meta.name not in _DISPLAY_ORDER:
   353→        _DISPLAY_ORDER.append(meta.name)
   354→    JUDGMENT_DETECTORS = frozenset(
   355→        name for name, m in DETECTORS.items() if m.needs_judgment
   356→    )
   357→    for cb in _on_register_callbacks:
   358→        cb()
   359→
   360→
   361→def reset_registered_detectors() -> None:
   362→    """Reset runtime-added detector registrations to built-in defaults."""
   363→    global JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   364→    DETECTORS.clear()
   365→    DETECTORS.update(_BASE_DETECTORS)
   366→    _DISPLAY_ORDER.clear()
   367→    _DISPLAY_ORDER.extend(_BASE_DISPLAY_ORDER)
   368→    JUDGMENT_DETECTORS = _BASE_JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   369→    for cb in _on_register_callbacks:
   370→        cb()
   371→
   372→
   373→def detector_names() -> list[str]:
   374→    """All registered detector names, sorted."""
   375→    return sorted(DETECTORS.keys())
   376→
   377→
   378→def display_order() -> list[str]:
   379→    """Canonical display order for terminal output."""
   380→    return list(_DISPLAY_ORDER)
   381→
   382→
   383→_ACTION_PRIORITY = {"auto_fix": 0, "reorganize": 1, "refactor": 2, "manual_fix": 3}
   384→_ACTION_LABELS = {
   385→    "auto_fix": "fix",
   386→    "reorganize": "move",
   387→    "refactor": "refactor",
   388→    "manual_fix": "manual",
   389→}
   390→
   391→
   392→def dimension_action_type(dim_name: str) -> str:
   393→    """Return a compact action type label for a dimension based on its detectors."""
   394→    best = "manual"
   395→    best_pri = 99
   396→    for d in DETECTORS.values():
   397→        if d.dimension == dim_name:
   398→            pri = _ACTION_PRIORITY.get(d.action_type, 99)
   399→            if pri < best_pri:
   400→                best_pri = pri
   401→                best = d.action_type
   402→    return _ACTION_LABELS.get(best, "manual")
   403→
   404→
   405→def detector_tools() -> dict[str, dict]:
   406→    """Build detector tool metadata keyed by detector name."""
   407→    result = {}
   408→    for name, d in DETECTORS.items():
   409→        entry: dict = {
   410→            "fixers": list(d.fixers),
   411→            "action_type": d.action_type,
   412→        }
   413→        if d.tool:
   414→            entry["tool"] = d.tool
   415→        if d.guidance:
   416→            entry["guidance"] = d.guidance
   417→        result[name] = entry
   418→    return result
   419→

    17→def _write_py(tmp_path: Path, code: str, filename: str = "test_mod.py") -> str:
    18→    """Write a Python file and return its path as a string."""
    19→    f = tmp_path / filename
    20→    f.write_text(textwrap.dedent(code))
    21→    return str(f)

    27→class TestExtractPyFunctions:
    28→    def test_simple_function(self, tmp_path):
    29→        fp = _write_py(
    30→            tmp_path,
    31→            """\
    32→            def greet(name):
    33→                greeting = f"Hello, {name}"
    34→                return greeting
    35→        """,
    36→        )
    37→        funcs = extract_py_functions(fp)
    38→        assert len(funcs) >= 1
    39→        fn = funcs[0]
    40→        assert fn.name == "greet"

    42→        assert fn.loc >= 3

   203→class TestNormalizePyBody:
   204→    def test_strips_comments(self):
   205→        body = textwrap.dedent("""\
   206→            def foo():
   207→                # a comment
   208→                x = 1
   209→                return x
   210→        """)
   211→        result = normalize_py_body(body)
   212→        assert "# a comment" not in result
   213→        assert "x = 1" in result
   214→
   215→    def test_strips_docstrings(self):
   216→        body = textwrap.dedent('''\
   217→            def foo():
   218→                """This is a docstring."""
   219→                x = 1
   220→                return x
   221→        ''')
   222→        result = normalize_py_body(body)
   223→        assert "docstring" not in result
   224→        assert "x = 1" in result
   225→
   226→    def test_strips_print_and_logging(self):
   227→        body = textwrap.dedent("""\
   228→            def foo():
   229→                x = 1
   230→                print(x)
   231→                logging.info("done")
   232→                return x
   233→        """)
   234→        result = normalize_py_body(body)
   235→        assert "print" not in result
   236→        assert "logging" not in result
   237→
   238→    def test_strips_blank_lines(self):
   239→        body = "def foo():\n    x = 1\n\n    return x\n"
   240→        result = normalize_py_body(body)
   241→        lines = result.splitlines()
   242→        assert all(line.strip() for line in lines)

   251→        methods = "\n".join(

   254→            for i in range(10)

   256→        code = f"class BigClass:\n{methods}\n"

   259→        classes = extract_py_classes(tmp_path)

   263→        assert cls.loc >= 50
   264→        assert len(cls.methods) >= 10

   271→            for i in range(8)
   272→        )
   273→        code = f"class WithAttrs:\n    def __init__(self):\n{body}\n\n{methods}\n"
   274→        fp = tmp_path / "attrs.py"

   276→        classes = extract_py_classes(tmp_path)

   301→            # Mixins are excluded from base_classes
   302→            assert "Parent" in cls.base_classes
   303→            assert "SomeMixin" not in cls.base_classes
   304→
   305→
   306→# ── detect_passthrough_functions ──────────────────────────
   307→
   308→
   309→class TestDetectPassthrough:
   310→    def test_passthrough_detected(self, tmp_path):
   311→        fp = tmp_path / "pt.py"
   312→        fp.write_text(
   313→            textwrap.dedent("""\
   314→            def wrapper(a, b, c, d, e):
   315→                return inner(a=a, b=b, c=c, d=d, e=e)
   316→        """)
   317→        )
   318→        entries = detect_passthrough_functions(tmp_path)
   319→        if entries:
   320→            assert entries[0]["function"] == "wrapper"
   321→            assert entries[0]["passthrough"] >= 4
   322→
   323→    def test_non_passthrough_not_flagged(self, tmp_path):
   324→        fp = tmp_path / "real.py"
   325→        fp.write_text(
   326→            textwrap.dedent("""\
   327→            def transform(a, b, c, d):
   328→                x = a + b
   329→                y = c * d
   330→                return x + y
   331→        """)
   332→        )
   333→        entries = detect_passthrough_functions(tmp_path)
   334→        names = [e["function"] for e in entries]
   335→        assert "transform" not in names
   336→
   337→    def test_too_few_params_not_flagged(self, tmp_path):
   338→        fp = tmp_path / "few.py"
   339→        fp.write_text(
   340→            textwrap.dedent("""\
   341→            def small(a, b):
   342→                return inner(a=a, b=b)
   343→        """)
   344→        )
   345→        entries = detect_passthrough_functions(tmp_path)
   346→        names = [e["function"] for e in entries]
   347→        assert "small" not in names
   348→

1→"""TypeScript function-level smell detectors — monster functions, dead functions, etc."""
     2→
     3→import re
     4→
     5→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_helpers import (
     6→    _strip_ts_comments,
     7→    _track_brace_body,
     8→    _ts_match_is_in_string,
     9→    scan_code,
    10→)
    11→
    12→_MAX_CATCH_BODY = 1000  # max characters to scan for catch block body
    13→_MAX_SWITCH_BODY_SCAN = 5000
    14→
    15→
    16→def _find_block_end(content: str, brace_start: int, max_scan: int) -> int | None:
    17→    depth = 0
    18→    for ci, ch, in_s in scan_code(content, brace_start, min(brace_start + max_scan, len(content))):
    19→        if in_s:
    20→            continue
    21→        if ch == "{":
    22→            depth += 1
    23→        elif ch == "}":
    24→            depth -= 1
    25→            if depth == 0:
    26→                return ci
    27→    return None
    28→
    29→
    30→def _find_function_start(line: str, next_lines: list[str]) -> str | None:
    31→    """Return function name for declarations/assignments, else None."""
    32→    stripped = line.strip()
    33→    if not stripped:
    34→        return None
    35→    if stripped.startswith(("interface ", "type ", "enum ", "class ")):
    36→        return None
    37→
    38→    declaration_match = re.match(
    39→        r"^(?:export\s+)?(?:default\s+)?(?:async\s+)?function\s+([A-Za-z_]\w*)\s*\(",
    40→        stripped,
    41→    )
    42→    if declaration_match:
    43→        return declaration_match.group(1)
    44→
    45→    assignment_match = re.match(
    46→        r"^(?:export\s+)?(?:const|let|var)\s+([A-Za-z_$][\w$]*)\b",
    47→        stripped,
    48→    )
    49→    if not assignment_match:
    50→        return None
    51→
    52→    combined = "\n".join(
    53→        [stripped] + [next_line.strip() for next_line in next_lines[:2]]
    54→    )
    55→    eq_pos = combined.find("=", assignment_match.end())
    56→    if eq_pos == -1:
    57→        return None
    58→    after_eq = combined[eq_pos + 1 :].lstrip()
    59→    if re.match(r"(?:async\s+)?\([^)]*\)\s*=>", after_eq):
    60→        return assignment_match.group(1)
    61→    if re.match(r"function\b", after_eq):
    62→        return assignment_match.group(1)
    63→    return None
    64→
    65→def _find_opening_brace_line(lines: list[str], start: int, *, window: int = 5) -> int | None:
    66→    for idx in range(start, min(start + window, len(lines))):
    67→        if "{" in lines[idx]:
    68→            return idx
    69→    return None
    70→
    71→
    72→def _detect_monster_functions(
    73→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
    74→):
    75→    """Find functions/components exceeding 150 LOC via brace-tracking.
    76→
    77→    Matches: function declarations, named arrow functions, and React components.
    78→    Skips: interfaces, types, enums, and objects/arrays.
    79→    """
    80→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
    81→        name = _find_function_start(line, lines[i + 1 : i + 3])
    82→        if not name:
    83→            continue
    84→
    85→        brace_line = _find_opening_brace_line(lines, i, window=5)
    86→        if brace_line is None:
    87→            continue
    88→
    89→        end_line = _track_brace_body(lines, brace_line, max_scan=2000)
    90→        if end_line is not None:
    91→            loc = end_line - i + 1
    92→            if loc > 150:
    93→                smell_counts["monster_function"].append(
    94→                    {
    95→                        "file": filepath,
    96→                        "line": i + 1,
    97→                        "content": f"{name}() — {loc} LOC",
    98→                    }
    99→                )
   100→

1→"""Multi-line smell detection helpers (brace-tracked).
     2→
     3→Shared utilities (string-aware scanning, brace tracking, comment stripping)
     4→plus a handful of smell detectors. Monster-function, dead-function,
     5→window-global, catch-return-default, and switch-no-default detectors live
     6→in _smell_detectors.py.
     7→"""
     8→
     9→from __future__ import annotations
    10→
    11→import re
    12→
    13→from desloppify.core.text_api import strip_c_style_comments
    14→from desloppify.languages.typescript.syntax.scanner import scan_code
    15→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_effects import (
    16→    detect_dead_useeffects as _detect_dead_useeffects_impl,
    17→)
    18→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_effects import (
    19→    detect_empty_if_chains as _detect_empty_if_chains_impl,
    20→)
    21→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_effects import (
    22→    detect_error_no_throw as _detect_error_no_throw_impl,
    23→)
    24→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_effects import (
    25→    detect_swallowed_errors as _detect_swallowed_errors_impl,
    26→)
    27→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_effects import (
    28→    track_brace_body as _track_brace_body_impl,
    29→)
    30→
    31→
    32→def _strip_ts_comments(text: str) -> str:
    33→    """Strip // and /* */ comments while preserving strings.
    34→
    35→    Delegates to the shared implementation in utils.py.
    36→    """
    37→    return strip_c_style_comments(text)
    38→
    39→
    40→def _ts_match_is_in_string(line: str, match_start: int) -> bool:
    41→    """Check if a match position falls inside a string literal or comment on a single line.
    42→
    43→    Mirrors Python's _match_is_in_string but for TS syntax (', ", `, //).
    44→    """
    45→    i = 0
    46→    in_str = None
    47→
    48→    while i < len(line):
    49→        if i == match_start:
    50→            return in_str is not None
    51→
    52→        ch = line[i]
    53→
    54→        # Escape sequences inside strings
    55→        if in_str and ch == "\\" and i + 1 < len(line):
    56→            i += 2
    57→            continue
    58→
    59→        if in_str:
    60→            if ch == in_str:
    61→                in_str = None
    62→            i += 1
    63→            continue
    64→
    65→        # Line comment — everything after is non-code
    66→        if ch == "/" and i + 1 < len(line) and line[i + 1] == "/":
    67→            return match_start > i
    68→
    69→        if ch in ("'", '"', "`"):
    70→            in_str = ch
    71→            i += 1
    72→            continue
    73→
    74→        i += 1
    75→
    76→    return False
    77→
    78→
    79→def _detect_async_no_await(
    80→    filepath: str, content: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
    81→):
    82→    """Find async functions that don't use await.
    83→
    84→    Algorithm: for each async declaration, track brace depth to find the function
    85→    body extent (up to 200 lines). Scan each line for 'await' within those braces.
    86→    If the opening brace closes (depth returns to 0) without seeing await, flag it.
    87→    """
    88→    async_re = re.compile(r"(?:async\s+function\s+(\w+)|(\w+)\s*=\s*async)")
    89→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
    90→        m = async_re.search(line)
    91→        if not m:
    92→            continue
    93→        name = m.group(1) or m.group(2)
    94→        brace_depth = 0
    95→        found_open = False
    96→        has_await = False
    97→        for j in range(i, min(i + 200, len(lines))):
    98→            body_line = lines[j]
    99→            prev_code_ch = ""
   100→            for _, ch, in_s in scan_code(body_line):
   101→                if in_s:
   102→                    continue
   103→                if ch == "/" and prev_code_ch == "/":
   104→                    break  # Rest of line is comment
   105→                elif ch == "{":
   106→                    brace_depth += 1
   107→                    found_open = True
   108→                elif ch == "}":
   109→                    brace_depth -= 1
   110→                prev_code_ch = ch
   111→            if "await " in body_line or "await\n" in body_line:
   112→                has_await = True
   113→            if found_open and brace_depth <= 0:
   114→                break
   115→
   116→        if found_open and not has_await:
   117→            smell_counts["async_no_await"].append(
   118→                {
   119→                    "file": filepath,
   120→                    "line": i + 1,
   121→                    "content": f"async {name or '(anonymous)'} has no await",
   122→                }
   123→            )
   124→
   125→
   126→def _detect_error_no_throw(
   127→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   128→):
   129→    """Find console.error calls not followed by throw or return."""
   130→    _detect_error_no_throw_impl(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   131→
   132→
   133→def _detect_empty_if_chains(
   134→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   135→):
   136→    """Find if/else chains where all branches are empty."""
   137→    _detect_empty_if_chains_impl(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   138→
   139→
   140→def _detect_dead_useeffects(
   141→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   142→):
   143→    """Find useEffect calls with empty or whitespace/comment-only bodies.
   144→
   145→    Algorithm: two-pass brace/paren tracking with string-escape awareness.
   146→    Pass 1: track paren depth to find the full useEffect(...) extent.
   147→    Pass 2: within that extent, find the arrow body ({...} after =>) using
   148→    brace depth, skipping characters inside string literals (', ", `).
   149→    Then strip comments from the body and check if anything remains.
   150→    """
   151→    _detect_dead_useeffects_impl(
   152→        filepath,
   153→        lines,
   154→        smell_counts,
   155→        scan_code_fn=scan_code,
   156→        strip_ts_comments_fn=_strip_ts_comments,
   157→    )
   158→
   159→
   160→def _detect_swallowed_errors(
   161→    filepath: str, content: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   162→):
   163→    """Find catch blocks whose only content is console.error/warn/log (swallowed errors).
   164→
   165→    Algorithm: regex-find each `catch(...) {`, then track brace depth with
   166→    string-escape awareness to extract the catch body (up to 500 chars).
   167→    Strip comments, split into statements, and check if every statement
   168→    is a console.error/warn/log call.
   169→    """
   170→    _detect_swallowed_errors_impl(
   171→        filepath,
   172→        content,
   173→        lines,
   174→        smell_counts,
   175→        scan_code_fn=scan_code,
   176→        strip_ts_comments_fn=_strip_ts_comments,
   177→    )
   178→
   179→
   180→def _track_brace_body(
   181→    lines: list[str], start_line: int, *, max_scan: int = 2000
   182→) -> int | None:
   183→    """Find the closing brace that matches the first opening brace from start_line.
   184→
   185→    Tracks brace depth with string-literal awareness (', ", `).
   186→    Returns the line index of the closing brace, or None if not found.
   187→    """
   188→    return _track_brace_body_impl(
   189→        lines,
   190→        start_line,
   191→        scan_code_fn=scan_code,
   192→        max_scan=max_scan,
   193→    )
   194→
```

1→"""Text-oriented utility helpers split from the main utils facade."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import os
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→
     8→from desloppify.core.runtime_state import current_runtime_context
     9→
    10→_DEFAULT_PROJECT_ROOT = Path(os.environ.get("DESLOPPIFY_ROOT", Path.cwd())).resolve()
    11→
    12→# Legacy module-level constant — kept for backwards compat, but prefer
    13→# get_project_root() which respects RuntimeContext overrides.
    14→PROJECT_ROOT = _DEFAULT_PROJECT_ROOT
    15→
    16→
    17→def get_project_root() -> Path:
    18→    """Return the active project root, checking RuntimeContext first.
    19→
    20→    Tests set ``RuntimeContext.project_root`` to point at a tmp directory.
    21→    Production code uses the process-level default from $DESLOPPIFY_ROOT / cwd.
    22→    """
    23→    override = current_runtime_context().project_root
    24→    if override is not None:
    25→        return override
    26→    return _DEFAULT_PROJECT_ROOT
    27→
    28→
    29→def read_code_snippet(
    30→    filepath: str,
    31→    line: int,
    32→    context: int = 1,
    33→    *,
    34→    project_root: Path | str | None = None,
    35→) -> str | None:
    36→    """Read ±context lines around a line number. Returns formatted string or None."""
    37→    try:
    38→        root = (
    39→            Path(project_root).resolve()
    40→            if project_root is not None
    41→            # Runtime-aware default; preserves RuntimeContext/project-root overrides.
    42→            else get_project_root()
    43→        )
    44→        full = Path(filepath)
    45→        if not full.is_absolute():
    46→            full = root / full
    47→        content = full.read_text(errors="replace")
    48→    except OSError:
    49→        return None
    50→    lines = content.splitlines()
    51→    if line < 1 or line > len(lines):
    52→        return None
    53→    start = max(0, line - 1 - context)
    54→    end = min(len(lines), line + context)
    55→    parts = []
    56→    for i in range(start, end):
    57→        ln = i + 1
    58→        marker = "→" if ln == line else " "
    59→        text = lines[i]
    60→        if len(text) > 120:
    61→            text = text[:117] + "..."
    62→        parts.append(f"    {marker} {ln:>4} │ {text}")
    63→    return "\n".join(parts)
    64→
    65→
    66→def get_area(filepath: str, *, min_depth: int = 2) -> str:
    67→    """Derive an area name from a file path (generic: first 2 path components).
    68→
    69→    *min_depth* controls the minimum number of path components required before
    70→    the two-component area is returned.  The default (2) means a path like
    71→    ``"a/b"`` already qualifies.  Python uses ``min_depth=3`` so that
    72→    ``"pkg/mod.py"`` returns just ``"pkg"`` while ``"pkg/sub/mod.py"`` returns
    73→    ``"pkg/sub"``.
    74→    """
    75→    text = (filepath or "").strip()
    76→    if not text:
    77→        return "(unknown)"
    78→    parts = Path(text).parts
    79→    if not parts:
    80→        return "(unknown)"
    81→    return "/".join(parts[:2]) if len(parts) >= min_depth else parts[0]
    82→
    83→
    84→def strip_c_style_comments(text: str) -> str:
    85→    """Strip // and /* */ comments while preserving string literals."""
    86→    result: list[str] = []
    87→    i = 0
    88→    in_str = None
    89→    while i < len(text):
    90→        ch = text[i]
    91→        if in_str:
    92→            if ch == "\\" and i + 1 < len(text):
    93→                result.append(text[i : i + 2])
    94→                i += 2
    95→                continue
    96→            if ch == in_str:
    97→                in_str = None
    98→            result.append(ch)
    99→            i += 1
   100→        elif ch in ('"', "'", "`"):
   101→            in_str = ch
   102→            result.append(ch)
   103→            i += 1
   104→        elif ch == "/" and i + 1 < len(text):
   105→            if text[i + 1] == "/":
   106→                nl = text.find("\n", i)
   107→                if nl == -1:
   108→                    break
   109→                i = nl
   110→            elif text[i + 1] == "*":
   111→                end = text.find("*/", i + 2)
   112→                if end == -1:
   113→                    break
   114→                i = end + 2
   115→            else:
   116→                result.append(ch)
   117→                i += 1
   118→        else:
   119→            result.append(ch)
   120→            i += 1
   121→    return "".join(result)
   122→
   123→
   124→def is_numeric(value: object) -> bool:
   125→    """Return True if *value* is an int or float but NOT a bool.
   126→
   127→    Python's ``bool`` is a subclass of ``int``, so ``isinstance(True, int)``
   128→    is ``True``.  Many JSON-derived payloads need to distinguish real numbers
   129→    from booleans; this helper centralises that guard.
   130→    """
   131→    return isinstance(value, int | float) and not isinstance(value, bool)
   132→
   133→
   134→__all__ = [
   135→    "PROJECT_ROOT",
   136→    "get_area",
   137→    "get_project_root",
   138→    "is_numeric",
   139→    "read_code_snippet",
   140→    "strip_c_style_comments",
   141→]
   142→

    10→
    11→# Import parsing helpers

    13→    r"^\s*(?:from\s+([\w.]+)\s+import\s+(\w+)|import\s+([\w.]+))", re.MULTILINE

    15→
    16→ASSERT_PATTERNS = [
    17→    re.compile(p)

    49→def resolve_import_spec(
    50→    spec: str, test_path: str, production_files: set[str]
    51→) -> str | None:
    52→    """Best-effort module-spec to source-file resolution for direct imports."""
    53→    module_path = spec.strip().replace(".", "/")
    54→    if not module_path or module_path.startswith("/"):
    55→        return None
    56→
    57→    candidates = (
    58→        f"{module_path}.py",
    59→        f"{module_path}/__init__.py",
    60→    )
    61→    for candidate in candidates:
    62→        if candidate in production_files:
    63→            return candidate
    64→        if test_path:
    65→            sibling = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(test_path), candidate)
    66→            if sibling in production_files:
    67→                return sibling
    68→    return None
    69→
    70→
    71→def resolve_barrel_reexports(_filepath: str, _production_files: set[str]) -> set[str]:

    76→def parse_test_import_specs(content: str) -> list[str]:
    77→    """Extract import specs from Python test content.
    78→
    79→    For ``from package import name``, emits both ``package`` and
    80→    ``package.name`` so that submodule imports (e.g.
    81→    ``from desloppify.engine._state import filtering``) resolve to
    82→    the submodule file rather than just the package ``__init__.py``.
    83→    """
    84→    specs: list[str] = []
    85→    for m in PY_IMPORT_RE.finditer(content):
    86→        if m.group(3):
    87→            # Plain ``import X.Y.Z``
    88→            specs.append(m.group(3))
    89→        elif m.group(1):
    90→            package = m.group(1)
    91→            imported_name = m.group(2)
    92→            specs.append(package)
    93→            if imported_name:
    94→                specs.append(f"{package}.{imported_name}")
    95→    return specs
    96→

Perfect. Now let me create a comprehensive summary document of all findings:

## Summary: Docstring Detection & Handling in Desloppify

Based on my thorough search of the desloppify codebase, I found **no evidence of any detector that flags docstrings for removal or removal of docstrings by fixers**. Instead, I found the opposite: docstrings are handled intelligently as metadata to skip or understand code context.

### Key Findings:

#### 1. **Docstring Helper Function** 
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/python/detectors/smells_ast/_shared.py` (lines 17-21)

```python
def _is_docstring(stmt: ast.AST) -> bool:
    """Check whether a statement is a docstring expression."""
    return isinstance(stmt, ast.Expr) and isinstance(
        stmt.value, ast.Constant | ast.JoinedStr
    )
```

**Purpose:** This helper detects docstring expressions in the AST to allow detectors to *skip over* or understand function bodies properly.

#### 2. **Docstring Stripping in Function Body Normalization**
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/python/extractors.py` (lines 89-116)

Function: `normalize_py_body(body: str) -> str`

This function strips docstrings, comments, and logging calls from normalized function bodies **for comparison/hashing purposes only**, not for code modification. This is used for:
- Body hashing (MD5 digest for detecting duplicate functions)
- Normalized representation for cross-file duplication detection

**Key lines:**
- Lines 101-106: Docstring detection and skipping
- The function is exported in `__all__` (line 200)
- Used internally for function extraction metrics, not for code generation

#### 3. **Dead Function Detector Uses Docstring Info**
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/python/detectors/smells_ast/_node_detectors.py` (lines 35-67)

Function: `_detect_dead_functions(filepath: str, node: ast.AST) -> list[dict]`

**Lines 50-66** explicitly handle docstrings:
```python
elif len(body) == 2:
    first, second = body
    if not _is_docstring(first):
        return []
    if isinstance(second, ast.Pass):
        desc = "docstring + pass"
    elif _is_return_none(second):
        desc = "docstring + return None"
    else:
        return []
    return [{...}]
```

This detector flags functions that are essentially dead (only contain a docstring and pass/return None), but does **NOT remove the docstring**—it just flags the function as having trivial content.

#### 4. **Noop Function Detector Skips Docstrings**
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/python/detectors/smells_ast/_tree_quality_detectors.py` (lines 142-209)

Function: `_detect_noop_function()`

**Lines 180-183:**
```python
# Strip leading docstring
if body and _is_docstring(body[0]):
    body = body[1:]
```

This *skips* the docstring when analyzing whether a function body is a no-op. It doesn't remove docstrings; it just ignores them when counting statement count for noop classification.

#### 5. **Python Test Coverage Comment Stripping**
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/python/test_coverage.py` (lines 144-162)

Functions: `strip_comments()` and `_strip_py_comment()`

These strip **Python inline comments** (starting with `#`) for test coverage analysis, but docstrings (triple quotes) are NOT addressed here because docstrings are valid code, not comments.

#### 6. **TypeScript Comment Stripping**
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/_internal/text_utils.py` (lines 84-121)

Function: `strip_c_style_comments(text: str) -> str`

Strips `//` and `/* */` comments while preserving string literals. Used for:
- TypeScript dead useEffect detection
- TypeScript empty if-chain detection
- TypeScript swallowed error detection

**Used by:**
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/typescript/detectors/_smell_helpers.py` (lines 32-37)

### No Python Auto-Fixers Exist
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/python/fixers/__init__.py`

```python
"""Python auto-fixers for mechanical cleanup tasks.

No fixers implemented yet. Add Python-specific fixers here.
"""
```

There are NO Python fixers at all. All fixers are TypeScript-only (in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/typescript/fixers/`):
- `imports.py`
- `logs.py`
- `if_chain.py`
- `useeffect.py`
- `params.py`
- `vars.py`
- `import_rewrite.py`
- `syntax_scan.py`
- `common.py`
- `fixer_io.py`

### Detector Registry
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/registry.py`

The canonical detector registry (lines 60-329) lists all detectors and their fixers. **No detector mentions docstrings**. Fixers are limited to:
- `unused-imports`, `unused-vars`, `unused-params`
- `debug-logs`
- `dead-useeffect`, `empty-if-chain`

### Conclusion

**Docstrings are never removed by desloppify.** The codebase uses docstring detection (`_is_docstring`) strategically to:
1. **Skip them** when analyzing noop functions (don't count as meaningful statements)
2. **Detect them** in dead function scenarios (functions with only docstring + pass)
3. **Strip them** from normalized body hashes (for comparison metrics, not code modification)

All docstring-related operations are **read-only analysis**, not code generation.

1→<!-- desloppify-begin -->
     2→<!-- desloppify-skill-version: 2 -->
     3→---
     4→name: desloppify
     5→description: >
     6→  Codebase health scanner and technical debt tracker. Use when the user asks
     7→  about code quality, technical debt, dead code, large files, god classes,
     8→  duplicate functions, code smells, naming issues, import cycles, or coupling
     9→  problems. Also use when asked for a health score, what to fix next, or to
    10→  create a cleanup plan. Supports 28 languages.
    11→allowed-tools: Bash(desloppify *)
    12→---
    13→
    14→# Desloppify
    15→
    16→## 1. Your Job
    17→
    18→Maximise the **strict score** honestly. Your main cycle: **scan → plan → execute → rescan**. Follow the scan output's **INSTRUCTIONS FOR AGENTS** — don't substitute your own analysis.
    19→
    20→**Don't be lazy.** Do large refactors and small detailed fixes with equal energy. If it takes touching 20 files, touch 20 files. If it's a one-line change, make it. No task is too big or too small — fix things properly, not minimally.
    21→
    22→## 2. The Workflow
    23→
    24→Three phases, repeated as a cycle.
    25→
    26→### Phase 1: Scan and review — understand the codebase
    27→
    28→```bash
    29→desloppify scan --path .       # analyse the codebase
    30→desloppify status              # check scores — are we at target?
    31→```
    32→
    33→The scan will tell you if subjective dimensions need review. Follow its instructions. To trigger a review manually:
    34→```bash
    35→desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import
    36→```
    37→
    38→### Phase 2: Plan — decide what to work on
    39→
    40→After reviews, triage stages and plan creation appear as queue items in `next`. Complete them in order:
    41→```bash
    42→desloppify next                                        # shows the next workflow step
    43→desloppify plan triage --stage observe --report "themes and root causes..."
    44→desloppify plan triage --stage reflect --report "comparison against completed work..."
    45→desloppify plan triage --stage organize --report "summary of priorities..."
    46→desloppify plan triage --complete --strategy "execution plan..."
    47→```
    48→
    49→Then shape the queue. **The plan shapes everything `next` gives you** — don't skip this step.
    50→
    51→```bash
    52→desloppify plan                          # see the full ordered queue
    53→desloppify plan move <pat> top           # reorder — what unblocks the most?
    54→desloppify plan cluster create <name>    # group related issues to batch-fix
    55→desloppify plan focus <cluster>          # scope next to one cluster
    56→desloppify plan skip <pat>              # defer — hide from next
    57→```
    58→
    59→More plan commands:
    60→```bash
    61→desloppify plan move <cluster> top       # move all cluster members at once
    62→desloppify plan move <a> <b> top         # mix clusters + findings in one move
    63→desloppify plan move <pat> before -t X   # position relative to another item/cluster
    64→desloppify plan cluster move a,b top     # move multiple clusters as one block
    65→desloppify plan done <pat>               # mark complete
    66→desloppify plan reopen <pat>             # reopen
    67→```
    68→
    69→### Phase 3: Execute — grind the queue to completion
    70→
    71→Trust the plan and execute. Don't rescan mid-queue — finish the queue first.
    72→
    73→**Branch first.** Create a dedicated branch for health work — never commit directly to main:
    74→```bash
    75→git checkout -b desloppify/code-health    # or desloppify/<focus-area>
    76→```
    77→
    78→**Set up commit tracking.** If you have a PR, link it for auto-updated descriptions:
    79→```bash
    80→desloppify config set commit_pr 42        # PR number for auto-updates
    81→```
    82→
    83→**The loop:**
    84→```
    85→1. desloppify next              ← what to fix next
    86→2. Fix the issue in code
    87→3. Resolve it (next shows you the exact command including required attestation)
    88→4. When you have a logical batch, commit:
    89→   git add <files> && git commit -m "desloppify: fix 3 deferred_import findings"
    90→5. Record the commit:
    91→   desloppify plan commit-log record      # moves findings uncommitted → committed, updates PR
    92→6. Push periodically:
    93→   git push -u origin desloppify/code-health
    94→7. Repeat until the queue is empty
    95→```
    96→
    97→Score may temporarily drop after fixes — cascade effects are normal, keep going.
    98→If `next` suggests an auto-fixer, run `desloppify autofix <fixer> --dry-run` to preview, then apply.
    99→
   100→**When the queue is clear, go back to Phase 1.** New issues will surface, cascades will have resolved, priorities will have shifted. This is the cycle.
   101→
   102→### Other useful commands
   103→
   104→```bash
   105→desloppify next --count 5                         # top 5 priorities
   106→desloppify next --cluster <name>                  # drill into a cluster
   107→desloppify show <pattern>                         # filter by file/detector/ID
   108→desloppify show --status open                     # all open findings
   109→desloppify plan skip --permanent "<id>" --note "reason" --attest "..." # accept debt
   110→desloppify exclude <path>                         # exclude a directory from scanning
   111→desloppify config show                            # show all config including excludes
   112→desloppify scan --path . --reset-subjective       # reset subjective baseline to 0
   113→```
   114→
   115→## 3. Reference
   116→
   117→### How scoring works
   118→
   119→Overall score = **40% mechanical** + **60% subjective**.
   120→
   121→- **Mechanical (40%)**: auto-detected issues — duplication, dead code, smells, unused imports, security. Fixed by changing code and rescanning.
   122→- **Subjective (60%)**: design quality review — naming, error handling, abstractions, clarity. Starts at **0%** until reviewed. The scan will prompt you when a review is needed.
   123→- **Strict score** is the north star: wontfix items count as open. The gap between overall and strict is your wontfix debt.
   124→- **Score types**: overall (lenient), strict (wontfix counts), objective (mechanical only), verified (confirmed fixes only).
   125→
   126→### Subjective reviews in detail
   127→
   128→- **Preferred**: `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import` — does everything in one command.
   129→- **Manual path**: `desloppify review --prepare` → review per dimension → `desloppify review --import file.json`.
   130→- Import first, fix after — import creates tracked state entries for correlation.
   131→- Integrity: reviewers score from evidence only. Scores hitting exact targets trigger auto-reset.
   132→- Even moderate scores (60-80) dramatically improve overall health.
   133→- Stale dimensions auto-surface in `next` — just follow the queue.
   134→
   135→### Commit tracking & branch workflow
   136→
   137→Work on a dedicated branch named `desloppify/<description>` (e.g., `desloppify/code-health`, `desloppify/fix-smells`). Never push health work directly to main.
   138→
   139→```bash
   140→desloppify config set commit_pr 42              # link to your PR
   141→desloppify plan commit-log                      # see uncommitted + committed status
   142→desloppify plan commit-log record               # record HEAD commit, update PR description
   143→desloppify plan commit-log record --note "why"  # with rationale
   144→desloppify plan commit-log record --only "smells::*"  # record specific findings only
   145→desloppify plan commit-log history              # show commit records
   146→desloppify plan commit-log pr                   # preview PR body markdown
   147→desloppify config set commit_tracking_enabled false  # disable guidance
   148→```
   149→
   150→After resolving findings as `fixed`, the tool shows uncommitted work, committed history, and a suggested commit message. After committing externally, run `record` to move findings from uncommitted to committed and auto-update the linked PR description.
   151→
   152→### Key concepts
   153→
   154→- **Tiers**: T1 auto-fix → T2 quick manual → T3 judgment call → T4 major refactor.
   155→- **Auto-clusters**: related findings are auto-grouped in `next`. Drill in with `next --cluster <name>`.
   156→- **Zones**: production/script (scored), test/config/generated/vendor (not scored). Fix with `zone set`.
   157→- **Wontfix cost**: widens the lenient↔strict gap. Challenge past decisions when the gap grows.
   158→- Score can temporarily drop after fixes (cascade effects are normal).
   159→
   160→## 4. Escalate Tool Issues Upstream
   161→
   162→When desloppify itself appears wrong or inconsistent:
   163→
   164→1. Capture a minimal repro (`command`, `path`, `expected`, `actual`).
   165→2. Open a GitHub issue in `peteromallet/desloppify`.
   166→3. If you can fix it safely, open a PR linked to that issue.
   167→4. If unsure whether it is tool bug vs user workflow, issue first, PR second.
   168→
   169→## Prerequisite
   170→
   171→`command -v desloppify >/dev/null 2>&1 && echo "desloppify: installed" || echo "NOT INSTALLED — run: pip install --upgrade git+https://github.com/peteromallet/desloppify.git"`
   172→
   173→<!-- desloppify-end -->
   174→
```

1→## Comments and docs
     2→
     3→- **Keep** existing docstrings/JSDoc — only remove if clearly stale or wrong.
     4→- **Don't add** new docs to code you didn't write — unless matching an existing codebase pattern.
     5→
     6→## Claude Code Overlay
     7→
     8→Use Claude subagents for subjective scoring work. **Do not use `--runner codex`** — use Claude subagents exclusively.
     9→
    10→### Subjective review
    11→
    12→Run `desloppify review --prepare` first to generate review data, then use Claude subagents:
    13→
    14→1. **Prepare**: `desloppify review --prepare` — writes `query.json` and `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json`.
    15→2. **Launch subagents**: Split the review across N parallel Claude subagents (one message, multiple Task calls). Each agent reviews a subset of dimensions.
    16→3. **Merge & import**: Merge agent outputs, then `desloppify review --import merged.json --manual-override --attest "Claude subagents ran blind reviews against review_packet_blind.json" --scan-after-import`.
    17→
    18→#### How to split dimensions across subagents
    19→
    20→- Read `dimension_prompts` from `query.json` — it lists all dimensions with their definitions and seed files.
    21→- Read `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json` for the blind packet (no score targets, no anchoring data).
    22→- Group dimensions into 3-4 batches by theme (e.g., architecture, code quality, testing, conventions).
    23→- Launch one Task agent per batch. Each agent gets:
    24→  - The codebase path and list of dimensions to score
    25→  - The blind packet path to read
    26→  - The output JSON schema (assessments + findings)
    27→  - Instruction to score from code evidence only, not from targets
    28→- Each agent writes output to a separate file. Merge assessments (average overlapping dimension scores) and concatenate findings.
    29→- Import first, fix after — import creates tracked state entries for correlation.
    30→
    31→### Subagent rules
    32→
    33→1. Use the Task tool with `subagent_type: "general-purpose"` for review agents.
    34→2. Each agent must be context-isolated — do not pass conversation history or score targets.
    35→3. Agents must consume `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json` (not full `query.json`) to avoid score anchoring.
    36→4. Score from evidence only; do not anchor to target thresholds. When mixed, score lower.
    37→5. Return machine-readable JSON matching the format in the base skill doc.
    38→6. `findings` MUST match the schema in the blind packet's `system_prompt`. Use `"findings": []` when no defects found.
    39→7. Import is fail-closed: invalid findings abort unless `--allow-partial` is passed.
    40→
    41→<!-- desloppify-overlay: claude -->
    42→<!-- desloppify-end -->
    43→
```

    47→

If an item is genuinely blocked or wrong, use `plan move` to reorder or `plan skip` with a note — don't silently move on.
    49→
