   137→
   138→1. **Preferred**: `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import` — does everything in one command.
   139→2. **Claude cloud path**: `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude` → use generated `claude_launch_prompt.md` + `review_result.template.json` → run printed `desloppify review --external-submit --session-id <id> --import <file>`.
   140→3. **Manual path**: split dimensions across N subagents (one message, multiple Task calls), merge outputs, then `desloppify review --import findings.json`.
   141→
   142→For the manual path:
   143→- Read `dimension_prompts` from `query.json` for dimension definitions.
   144→- Give each agent the codebase path, dimensions, and output format. Let agents decide what to read.
   145→- Each agent writes output to a separate file. Merge assessments (average overlaps) and findings.
   146→- Import first, fix after — import creates tracked state for correlation.
   147→
   148→### Subagent rules
   149→
   150→1. Prefer delegating review tasks to a project subagent in `.claude/agents/`.
   151→2. Set `context: fork` so prior chat context does not leak into scoring.
   152→3. For blind reviews, consume `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json` instead of full `query.json`.
   153→4. Score from evidence only; do not anchor to target thresholds. When mixed, score lower.
   154→5. Return machine-readable JSON matching the format in the base skill doc. For `--external-submit`, include `session` from the generated template.
   155→6. `findings` MUST match `query.system_prompt` exactly. Use `"findings": []` when no defects found.
   156→7. Import is fail-closed: invalid findings abort unless `--allow-partial` is passed.
   157→
   158→<!-- desloppify-overlay: claude -->
   159→<!-- desloppify-end -->
   160→
```

1→## Cursor Overlay
     2→
     3→Cursor supports native subagents via `.cursor/agents/` definitions. Use them
     4→for context-isolated subjective reviews.
     5→
     6→### Parallel review
     7→
     8→Split dimensions across subagents so each reviewer scores independently.
     9→Define a reviewer agent in `.cursor/agents/desloppify-reviewer.md`:
    10→
    11→```markdown
    12→---
    13→name: desloppify-reviewer
    14→description: Scores subjective codebase quality dimensions for desloppify
    15→tools:
    16→  - read
    17→  - search
    18→---
    19→
    20→You are a code quality reviewer. You will be given a codebase path, a set of
    21→dimensions to score, and what each dimension means. Read the code, score each
    22→dimension 0-100 from evidence only, and return JSON in the required format.
    23→Do not anchor to target thresholds. When evidence is mixed, score lower and
    24→explain uncertainty.
    25→```
    26→
    27→Workflow:
    28→1. Read `dimension_prompts` from `query.json` for dimension definitions.
    29→2. Launch multiple reviewer subagents, each with a subset of dimensions.
    30→3. Each agent writes its output to a separate file.
    31→4. Merge assessments (average where dimensions overlap) and findings.
    32→5. Preferred local path (Codex runner): `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import`.
    33→6. Cursor/cloud/manual path:
    34→   - robust session flow (recommended): `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude`; use generated `claude_launch_prompt.md` + `review_result.template.json`, then run printed `desloppify review --external-submit --session-id <id> --import <file>`
    35→   - durable scored import (legacy): `desloppify review --import findings.json --attested-external --attest "I validated this review was completed without awareness of overall score and is unbiased."`
    36→   - findings-only fallback: `desloppify review --import findings.json`
    37→
    38→### Review integrity
    39→
    40→1. Do not use prior chat context, score history, or target-threshold anchoring while scoring.
    41→2. Score from evidence only; when evidence is mixed, score lower and explain uncertainty.
    42→3. Return machine-readable JSON only for review imports. For `--external-submit`, include `session` from the generated template:
    43→
    44→```json
    45→{
    46→  "session": {
    47→    "id": "<session_id_from_template>",
    48→    "token": "<session_token_from_template>"
    49→  },
    50→  "assessments": {
    51→    "naming_quality": 0,
    52→    "error_consistency": 0,
    53→    "abstraction_fit": 0,
    54→    "logic_clarity": 0,
    55→    "ai_generated_debt": 0
    56→  },
    57→  "findings": [
    58→    {
    59→      "dimension": "naming_quality",
    60→      "identifier": "short_id",
    61→      "summary": "one-line defect summary",
    62→      "related_files": ["relative/path/to/file.py"],
    63→      "evidence": ["specific code observation"],
    64→      "suggestion": "concrete fix recommendation",
    65→      "confidence": "high|medium|low"
    66→    }
    67→  ]
    68→}
    69→```
    70→4. `findings` MUST match `query.system_prompt` exactly. Use `"findings": []` only when no defects are found.
    71→5. Import is fail-closed by default: invalid/skipped findings abort `desloppify review --import` unless `--allow-partial` is explicitly passed.
    72→6. Assessment scores are auto-applied from trusted internal run-batches imports, or via Claude cloud session imports (`--external-start` + printed `--external-submit`). Legacy attested external import via `--attested-external` remains supported.
    73→
    74→<!-- desloppify-overlay: cursor -->
    75→<!-- desloppify-end -->
    76→
```

1→## Codex Overlay
     2→
     3→This is the canonical Codex overlay used by the README install command.
     4→
     5→1. Prefer first-class batch runs: `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import`.
     6→2. The command writes immutable packet snapshots under `.desloppify/review_packets/holistic_packet_*.json`; use those for reproducible retries.
     7→3. Keep reviewer input scoped to the immutable packet and the source files named in each batch.
     8→4. Do not use prior chat context, score history, narrative summaries, issue labels, or target-threshold anchoring while scoring.
     9→5. Assess every dimension listed in `query.dimensions`; never drop a requested dimension. If evidence is weak/mixed, score lower and explain uncertainty in findings.
    10→6. Return machine-readable JSON only for review imports. For Claude session submit (`--external-submit`), include `session` from the generated template:
    11→
    12→```json
    13→{
    14→  "session": {
    15→    "id": "<session_id_from_template>",
    16→    "token": "<session_token_from_template>"
    17→  },
    18→  "assessments": {
    19→    "<dimension_from_query>": 0
    20→  },
    21→  "findings": [
    22→    {
    23→      "dimension": "<dimension_from_query>",
    24→      "identifier": "short_id",
    25→      "summary": "one-line defect summary",
    26→      "related_files": ["relative/path/to/file.py"],
    27→      "evidence": ["specific code observation"],
    28→      "suggestion": "concrete fix recommendation",
    29→      "confidence": "high|medium|low"
    30→    }
    31→  ]
    32→}
    33→```
    34→
    35→7. `findings` MUST match `query.system_prompt` exactly (including `related_files`, `evidence`, and `suggestion`). Use `"findings": []` when no defects are found.
    36→8. Import is fail-closed by default: if any finding is invalid/skipped, `desloppify review --import` aborts unless `--allow-partial` is explicitly passed.
    37→9. Assessment scores are auto-applied from trusted internal run-batches imports, or via Claude cloud session imports (`desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude` then printed `--external-submit`). Legacy attested external import via `--attested-external` remains supported.
    38→10. Manual override is safety-scoped: you cannot combine it with `--allow-partial`, and provisional manual scores expire on the next `scan` unless replaced by trusted internal or attested-external imports.
    39→11. If a batch fails, retry only that slice with `desloppify review --run-batches --packet <packet.json> --only-batches <idxs>`.
    40→
    41→<!-- desloppify-overlay: codex -->
    42→<!-- desloppify-end -->
    43→
```

1→"""Skill-document versioning and install metadata helpers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import re
     6→from dataclasses import dataclass
     7→
     8→from desloppify.core.text_api import get_project_root
     9→
    10→# Bump this integer whenever docs/SKILL.md changes in a way that agents
    11→# should pick up (new commands, changed workflows, removed sections).
    12→SKILL_VERSION = 2
    13→
    14→SKILL_VERSION_RE = re.compile(r"<!--\s*desloppify-skill-version:\s*(\d+)\s*-->")
    15→SKILL_OVERLAY_RE = re.compile(r"<!--\s*desloppify-overlay:\s*(\w+)\s*-->")
    16→
    17→SKILL_BEGIN = "<!-- desloppify-begin -->"
    18→SKILL_END = "<!-- desloppify-end -->"
    19→
    20→# Locations where the skill doc might be installed, relative to project root.
    21→SKILL_SEARCH_PATHS = (
    22→    ".claude/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md",
    23→    ".opencode/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md",
    24→    "AGENTS.md",
    25→    "CLAUDE.md",
    26→    ".cursor/rules/desloppify.md",
    27→    ".github/copilot-instructions.md",
    28→)
    29→
    30→# Interface name → (target file, overlay filename, dedicated).
    31→# Dedicated files are overwritten entirely; shared files get section replacement.
    32→SKILL_TARGETS: dict[str, tuple[str, str, bool]] = {
    33→    "claude": (".claude/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md", "CLAUDE", True),
    34→    # OpenCode support added with thanks to @H3xKatana.
    35→    "opencode": (".opencode/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md", "OPENCODE", True),
    36→    "codex": ("AGENTS.md", "CODEX", False),
    37→    "cursor": (".cursor/rules/desloppify.md", "CURSOR", True),
    38→    "copilot": (".github/copilot-instructions.md", "COPILOT", False),
    39→    "windsurf": ("AGENTS.md", "WINDSURF", False),
    40→    "gemini": ("AGENTS.md", "GEMINI", False),
    41→}
    42→
    43→
    44→@dataclass
    45→class SkillInstall:
    46→    """Detected skill document installation."""
    47→
    48→    rel_path: str
    49→    version: int
    50→    overlay: str | None
    51→    stale: bool
    52→
    53→
    54→def find_installed_skill() -> SkillInstall | None:
    55→    """Find installed skill document metadata, or None."""
    56→    project_root = get_project_root()
    57→    for rel_path in SKILL_SEARCH_PATHS:
    58→        full = project_root / rel_path
    59→        if not full.is_file():
    60→            continue
    61→        try:
    62→            content = full.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
    63→        except OSError as exc:
    64→            _ = exc
    65→            continue
    66→        version_match = SKILL_VERSION_RE.search(content)
    67→        if not version_match:
    68→            continue
    69→        installed_version = int(version_match.group(1))
    70→        overlay_match = SKILL_OVERLAY_RE.search(content)
    71→        overlay = overlay_match.group(1) if overlay_match else None
    72→        return SkillInstall(
    73→            rel_path=rel_path,
    74→            version=installed_version,
    75→            overlay=overlay,
    76→            stale=installed_version < SKILL_VERSION,
    77→        )
    78→    return None
    79→
    80→
    81→def check_skill_version() -> str | None:
    82→    """Return a warning if installed skill doc is outdated."""
    83→    install = find_installed_skill()
    84→    if not install or not install.stale:
    85→        return None
    86→    return (
    87→        f"Your desloppify skill document is outdated "
    88→        f"(v{install.version}, current v{SKILL_VERSION}). "
    89→        "Run: desloppify update-skill"
    90→    )
    91→
    92→
    93→__all__ = [
    94→    "SKILL_VERSION",
    95→    "SKILL_VERSION_RE",
    96→    "SKILL_OVERLAY_RE",
    97→    "SKILL_BEGIN",
    98→    "SKILL_END",
    99→    "SKILL_SEARCH_PATHS",
   100→    "SKILL_TARGETS",
   101→    "SkillInstall",
   102→    "find_installed_skill",
   103→    "check_skill_version",
   104→]
   105→
```

1→"""update-skill command: install or update the desloppify skill document."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→import urllib.error
     7→import urllib.request
     8→
     9→from desloppify.core.path_io_api import safe_write_text
    10→from desloppify.core.paths_api import get_project_root
    11→from desloppify.core.skill_docs import (
    12→    SKILL_BEGIN,
    13→    SKILL_END,
    14→    SKILL_TARGETS,
    15→    SKILL_VERSION,
    16→    SKILL_VERSION_RE,
    17→    SkillInstall,
    18→    find_installed_skill,
    19→)
    20→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    21→
    22→_RAW_BASE = (
    23→    "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/peteromallet/desloppify/main/docs"
    24→)
    25→
    26→
    27→def _download(filename: str) -> str:
    28→    """Download a file from the desloppify docs directory on GitHub."""
    29→    url = f"{_RAW_BASE}/{filename}"
    30→    with urllib.request.urlopen(url, timeout=15) as resp:  # noqa: S310
    31→        return resp.read().decode("utf-8")
    32→
    33→
    34→def _build_section(skill_content: str, overlay_content: str | None) -> str:
    35→    """Assemble the complete skill section from downloaded parts."""
    36→    parts = [skill_content.rstrip()]
    37→    if overlay_content:
    38→        parts.append(overlay_content.rstrip())
    39→    return "\n\n".join(parts) + "\n"
    40→
    41→
    42→def _replace_section(file_content: str, new_section: str) -> str:
    43→    """Replace the desloppify section in a shared file, preserving surrounding content.
    44→
    45→    Uses first ``<!-- desloppify-begin -->`` and last ``<!-- desloppify-end -->``
    46→    so the overlay (which also has an end marker) is captured correctly.
    47→    """
    48→    begin = file_content.find(SKILL_BEGIN)
    49→    end = file_content.rfind(SKILL_END)
    50→    if begin == -1 or end == -1:
    51→        # No section markers — append (first install into existing shared file).
    52→        return file_content.rstrip() + "\n\n" + new_section
    53→
    54→    before = file_content[:begin]
    55→    after = file_content[end + len(SKILL_END):]
    56→    before = before.rstrip() + "\n\n" if before.strip() else ""
    57→    after = "\n" + after.lstrip("\n") if after.strip() else "\n"
    58→    return before + new_section + after
    59→
    60→
    61→def resolve_interface(
    62→    explicit: str | None = None,
    63→    install: SkillInstall | None = None,
    64→) -> str | None:
    65→    """Resolve which interface to update.
    66→
    67→    Uses the explicit argument if given, otherwise infers from an existing
    68→    install's overlay marker or file path.
    69→    """
    70→    if explicit:
    71→        return explicit.lower()
    72→
    73→    if install is None:
    74→        install = find_installed_skill()
    75→    if not install:
    76→        return None
    77→
    78→    if install.overlay:
    79→        return install.overlay.lower()
    80→
    81→    for name, (target, _overlay, _ded) in SKILL_TARGETS.items():
    82→        if target == install.rel_path:
    83→            return name
    84→    return None
    85→
    86→
    87→def update_installed_skill(interface: str) -> bool:
    88→    """Download and install the skill document for the given interface.
    89→
    90→    Returns True on success, False on failure. Prints status messages.
    91→    """
    92→    target_rel, overlay_name, dedicated = SKILL_TARGETS[interface]
    93→    target_path = get_project_root() / target_rel
    94→
    95→    print(colorize(f"Downloading skill document ({interface})...", "dim"))
    96→    try:
    97→        skill_content = _download("SKILL.md")
    98→        overlay_content = _download(f"{overlay_name}.md") if overlay_name else None
    99→    except (urllib.error.URLError, OSError) as exc:
   100→        print(colorize(f"Download failed: {exc}", "red"))
   101→        return False
   102→
   103→    if "desloppify-skill-version" not in skill_content:
   104→        print(colorize("Downloaded content doesn't look like a skill document.", "red"))
   105→        return False
   106→
   107→    new_section = _build_section(skill_content, overlay_content)
   108→
   109→    target_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   110→    if dedicated:
   111→        result = new_section
   112→    elif target_path.is_file():
   113→        existing = target_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
   114→        result = _replace_section(existing, new_section)
   115→    else:
   116→        result = new_section
   117→
   118→    safe_write_text(target_path, result)
   119→
   120→    version_match = SKILL_VERSION_RE.search(new_section)
   121→    version = version_match.group(1) if version_match else "?"
   122→    print(
   123→        colorize(
   124→            f"Updated {target_rel} (v{version}, tool expects v{SKILL_VERSION})",
   125→            "green",
   126→        )
   127→    )
   128→    return True
   129→
   130→
   131→def cmd_update_skill(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   132→    """Install or update the desloppify skill document."""
   133→    interface = resolve_interface(getattr(args, "interface", None))
   134→
   135→    if not interface:
   136→        print(colorize("No installed skill document found.", "yellow"))
   137→        print()
   138→        names = ", ".join(sorted(SKILL_TARGETS))
   139→        print(f"Install with: desloppify update-skill <{names}>")
   140→        return
   141→
   142→    if interface not in SKILL_TARGETS:
   143→        names = ", ".join(sorted(SKILL_TARGETS))
   144→        print(colorize(f"Unknown interface '{interface}'.", "red"))
   145→        print(f"Available: {names}")
   146→        return
   147→
   148→    update_installed_skill(interface)
   149→
```

1→## Windsurf Overlay
     2→
     3→Windsurf does not support spawning subagents from within a Cascade session.
     4→Parallel reviews require the user to open multiple Cascade panes manually.
     5→
     6→### Review workflow
     7→
     8→1. Preferred local path (Codex runner): `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import`.
     9→2. Windsurf/cloud path: run `desloppify review --prepare` to generate `query.json`.
    10→3. Ask the user to open additional Cascade panes for parallel review.
    11→   Suggest splitting dimensions across 2-3 panes (e.g., naming + clarity
    12→   in one, abstraction + error consistency in another).
    13→4. Each pane should score its assigned dimensions independently, reading
    14→   the codebase and `query.json`'s `dimension_prompts` for context.
    15→5. Each pane writes its output to a separate file.
    16→6. In the primary pane, merge assessments (average where dimensions overlap)
    17→   and findings, then import:
    18→   - robust session flow (recommended): `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude`; use generated `claude_launch_prompt.md` + `review_result.template.json`, then run printed `desloppify review --external-submit --session-id <id> --import <file>`
    19→   - durable scored import (legacy): `desloppify review --import findings.json --attested-external --attest "I validated this review was completed without awareness of overall score and is unbiased."`
    20→   - findings-only fallback: `desloppify review --import findings.json`
    21→
    22→If the user prefers a single-pane workflow, review all dimensions sequentially
    23→in one session. This is slower but still works.
    24→
    25→### Review integrity
    26→
    27→1. Do not use prior chat context, score history, or target-threshold anchoring while scoring.
    28→2. Score from evidence only; when evidence is mixed, score lower and explain uncertainty.
    29→3. Return machine-readable JSON only for review imports. For `--external-submit`, include `session` from the generated template:
    30→
    31→```json
    32→{
    33→  "session": {
    34→    "id": "<session_id_from_template>",
    35→    "token": "<session_token_from_template>"
    36→  },
    37→  "assessments": {
    38→    "naming_quality": 0,
    39→    "error_consistency": 0,
    40→    "abstraction_fit": 0,
    41→    "logic_clarity": 0,
    42→    "ai_generated_debt": 0
    43→  },
    44→  "findings": [
    45→    {
    46→      "dimension": "naming_quality",
    47→      "identifier": "short_id",
    48→      "summary": "one-line defect summary",
    49→      "related_files": ["relative/path/to/file.py"],
    50→      "evidence": ["specific code observation"],
    51→      "suggestion": "concrete fix recommendation",
    52→      "confidence": "high|medium|low"
    53→    }
    54→  ]
    55→}
    56→```
    57→4. `findings` MUST match `query.system_prompt` exactly. Use `"findings": []` only when no defects are found.
    58→5. Import is fail-closed by default: invalid/skipped findings abort `desloppify review --import` unless `--allow-partial` is explicitly passed.
    59→6. Assessment scores are auto-applied from trusted internal run-batches imports, or via Claude cloud session imports (`--external-start` + printed `--external-submit`). Legacy attested external import via `--attested-external` remains supported.
    60→
    61→<!-- desloppify-overlay: windsurf -->
    62→<!-- desloppify-end -->
    63→
```

1→"""Developer utilities."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→import ast
     7→import keyword
     8→import re
     9→
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.dev_scaffold_templates import build_scaffold_files
    11→from desloppify.core.text_api import PROJECT_ROOT
    12→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import safe_write_text
    13→from desloppify.core.exception_sets import CommandError
    14→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    15→
    16→
    17→def cmd_dev(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    18→    """Dispatch developer subcommands."""
    19→    action = getattr(args, "dev_action", None)
    20→    if action == "scaffold-lang":
    21→        try:
    22→            _cmd_scaffold_lang(args)
    23→        except ValueError as ex:
    24→            raise CommandError(str(ex)) from ex
    25→        return
    26→    raise CommandError("Unknown dev action. Use `desloppify dev scaffold-lang`.")
    27→
    28→
    29→def _normalize_lang_name(raw: str) -> str:
    30→    name = raw.strip().lower().replace("-", "_")
    31→    if not re.fullmatch(r"[a-z][a-z0-9_]*", name):
    32→        raise ValueError("language name must match [a-z][a-z0-9_]*")
    33→    if keyword.iskeyword(name):
    34→        raise ValueError(f"language name cannot be a Python keyword: {name}")
    35→    return name
    36→
    37→
    38→def _normalize_extensions(values: list[str] | None) -> list[str]:
    39→    out: list[str] = []
    40→    for val in values or []:
    41→        ext = val.strip()
    42→        if not ext:
    43→            continue
    44→        if not ext.startswith("."):
    45→            ext = "." + ext
    46→        if not re.fullmatch(r"\.[a-z0-9]+", ext):
    47→            raise ValueError(f"invalid extension: {val!r}")
    48→        out.append(ext)
    49→    if not out:
    50→        raise ValueError("at least one --extension is required")
    51→    seen: set[str] = set()
    52→    deduped: list[str] = []
    53→    for ext in out:
    54→        if ext not in seen:
    55→            seen.add(ext)
    56→            deduped.append(ext)
    57→    return deduped
    58→
    59→
    60→def _normalize_markers(values: list[str] | None) -> list[str]:
    61→    out: list[str] = []
    62→    for val in values or []:
    63→        marker = val.strip()
    64→        if marker and marker not in out:
    65→            out.append(marker)
    66→    return out
    67→
    68→
    69→def _class_name(lang_name: str) -> str:
    70→    return "".join(part.capitalize() for part in lang_name.split("_")) + "Config"
    71→
    72→
    73→def _template_files(
    74→    lang_name: str,
    75→    extensions: list[str],
    76→    markers: list[str],
    77→    default_src: str,
    78→) -> dict[str, str]:
    79→    class_name = _class_name(lang_name)
    80→    return build_scaffold_files(
    81→        lang_name=lang_name,
    82→        class_name=class_name,
    83→        extensions=extensions,
    84→        markers=markers,
    85→        default_src=default_src,
    86→    )
    87→
    88→
    89→def _render_array(items: list[str]) -> str:
    90→    return ", ".join(repr(x) for x in items)
    91→
    92→
    93→def _append_toml_array_item(text: str, key: str, value: str) -> str:
    94→    pattern = re.compile(
    95→        rf"(^\s*{re.escape(key)}\s*=\s*\[)(.*?)(\]\s*$)",
    96→        re.MULTILINE | re.DOTALL,
    97→    )
    98→    match = pattern.search(text)
    99→    if not match:
   100→        return text
   101→
   102→    raw = match.group(2).strip()
   103→    parsed = ast.literal_eval("[" + raw + "]") if raw else []
   104→    if not isinstance(parsed, list):
   105→        return text
   106→    if value in parsed:
   107→        return text
   108→
   109→    parsed.append(value)
   110→    replacement = f"{match.group(1)}{_render_array(parsed)}{match.group(3)}"
   111→    return text[: match.start()] + replacement + text[match.end() :]
   112→
   113→
   114→def _wire_pyproject(lang_name: str) -> bool:
   115→    pyproject_path = PROJECT_ROOT / "pyproject.toml"
   116→    if not pyproject_path.is_file():
   117→        return False
   118→
   119→    original = pyproject_path.read_text()
   120→    updated = original
   121→    updated = _append_toml_array_item(
   122→        updated,
   123→        "testpaths",
   124→        f"desloppify/languages/{lang_name}/tests",
   125→    )
   126→
   127→    if updated != original:
   128→        safe_write_text(pyproject_path, updated)
   129→        return True
   130→    return False
   131→
   132→
   133→def _cmd_scaffold_lang(args) -> None:
   134→    raw_name = getattr(args, "name", "")
   135→    lang_name = _normalize_lang_name(raw_name)
   136→    extensions = _normalize_extensions(getattr(args, "extension", None))
   137→    markers = _normalize_markers(getattr(args, "marker", None))
   138→    default_src = getattr(args, "default_src", "src") or "src"
   139→    force = bool(getattr(args, "force", False))
   140→    wire_pyproject = bool(getattr(args, "wire_pyproject", True))
   141→
   142→    lang_dir = PROJECT_ROOT / "desloppify" / "languages" / lang_name
   143→    if lang_dir.exists() and not force:
   144→        raise CommandError(
   145→            f"Language directory already exists: {lang_dir}. Use --force to overwrite."
   146→        )
   147→
   148→    files = _template_files(lang_name, extensions, markers, default_src)
   149→    for rel_path, content in files.items():
   150→        target = lang_dir / rel_path
   151→        target.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   152→        if target.exists() and not force:
   153→            continue
   154→        safe_write_text(target, content)
   155→
   156→    wired = _wire_pyproject(lang_name) if wire_pyproject else False
   157→
   158→    print(colorize(f"Scaffolded language plugin: {lang_name}", "green"))
   159→    print(f"  Path: {lang_dir}")
   160→    print(f"  Extensions: {', '.join(extensions)}")
   161→    print(f"  Markers: {', '.join(markers) if markers else '(none)'}")
   162→    print(f"  pyproject.toml updated: {'yes' if wired else 'no'}")
   163→    print(
   164→        colorize(
   165→            "  Next: implement real phases/commands/detectors and run pytest.", "dim"
   166→        )
   167→    )
   168→
```

1→## Gemini CLI Overlay
     2→
     3→Gemini CLI has experimental subagent support, but subagents currently run
     4→sequentially (not in parallel). Review dimensions one at a time.
     5→
     6→### Setup
     7→
     8→Enable subagents in Gemini CLI settings:
     9→```json
    10→{
    11→  "experimental": {
    12→    "enableAgents": true
    13→  }
    14→}
    15→```
    16→
    17→Optionally define a reviewer agent in `.gemini/agents/desloppify-reviewer.md`:
    18→
    19→```yaml
    20→---
    21→name: desloppify-reviewer
    22→description: Scores subjective codebase quality dimensions for desloppify
    23→kind: local
    24→tools:
    25→  - read_file
    26→  - search_code
    27→temperature: 0.2
    28→max_turns: 10
    29→---
    30→
    31→You are a code quality reviewer. You will be given a codebase path, a set of
    32→dimensions to score, and what each dimension means. Read the code, score each
    33→dimension 0-100 from evidence only, and return JSON in the required format.
    34→Do not anchor to target thresholds. When evidence is mixed, score lower and
    35→explain uncertainty.
    36→```
    37→
    38→### Review workflow
    39→
    40→1. Preferred local path (Codex runner): `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import`.
    41→2. Gemini/cloud path: run `desloppify review --prepare` to generate `query.json`.
    42→3. Invoke the reviewer agent for each group of dimensions sequentially.
    43→   Even without parallelism, isolating dimensions across separate agent
    44→   invocations prevents score bleed between concerns.
    45→4. Merge assessments and findings, then import:
    46→   - robust session flow (recommended): `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude`; use generated `claude_launch_prompt.md` + `review_result.template.json`, then run printed `desloppify review --external-submit --session-id <id> --import <file>`
    47→   - durable scored import (legacy): `desloppify review --import findings.json --attested-external --attest "I validated this review was completed without awareness of overall score and is unbiased."`
    48→   - findings-only fallback: `desloppify review --import findings.json`
    49→
    50→When Gemini CLI adds parallel subagent execution, split dimensions across
    51→concurrent agent calls instead.
    52→
    53→### Review integrity
    54→
    55→1. Do not use prior chat context, score history, or target-threshold anchoring while scoring.
    56→2. Score from evidence only; when evidence is mixed, score lower and explain uncertainty.
    57→3. Return machine-readable JSON only for review imports. For `--external-submit`, include `session` from the generated template:
    58→
    59→```json
    60→{
    61→  "session": {
    62→    "id": "<session_id_from_template>",
    63→    "token": "<session_token_from_template>"
    64→  },
    65→  "assessments": {
    66→    "naming_quality": 0,
    67→    "error_consistency": 0,
    68→    "abstraction_fit": 0,
    69→    "logic_clarity": 0,
    70→    "ai_generated_debt": 0
    71→  },
    72→  "findings": [
    73→    {
    74→      "dimension": "naming_quality",
    75→      "identifier": "short_id",
    76→      "summary": "one-line defect summary",
    77→      "related_files": ["relative/path/to/file.py"],
    78→      "evidence": ["specific code observation"],
    79→      "suggestion": "concrete fix recommendation",
    80→      "confidence": "high|medium|low"
    81→    }
    82→  ]
    83→}
    84→```
    85→4. `findings` MUST match `query.system_prompt` exactly. Use `"findings": []` only when no defects are found.
    86→5. Import is fail-closed by default: invalid/skipped findings abort `desloppify review --import` unless `--allow-partial` is explicitly passed.
    87→6. Assessment scores are auto-applied from trusted internal run-batches imports, or via Claude cloud session imports (`--external-start` + printed `--external-submit`). Legacy attested external import via `--attested-external` remains supported.
    88→
    89→<!-- desloppify-overlay: gemini -->
    90→<!-- desloppify-end -->
    91→
```

1→<!-- desloppify-begin -->
     2→<!-- desloppify-skill-version: 2 -->
     3→---
     4→name: desloppify
     5→description: >
     6→  Codebase health scanner and technical debt tracker. Use when the user asks
     7→  about code quality, technical debt, dead code, large files, god classes,
     8→  duplicate functions, code smells, naming issues, import cycles, or coupling
     9→  problems. Also use when asked for a health score, what to fix next, or to
    10→  create a cleanup plan. Supports 28 languages.
    11→allowed-tools: Bash(desloppify *)
    12→---
    13→
    14→# Desloppify
    15→
    16→## 1. Your Job
    17→
    18→Maximise the **strict score** honestly. Your main cycle: **scan → plan → execute → rescan**. Follow the scan output's **INSTRUCTIONS FOR AGENTS** — don't substitute your own analysis.
    19→
    20→**Don't be lazy.** Do large refactors and small detailed fixes with equal energy. If it takes touching 20 files, touch 20 files. If it's a one-line change, make it. No task is too big or too small — fix things properly, not minimally.
    21→
    22→## 2. The Workflow
    23→
    24→Three phases, repeated as a cycle.
    25→
    26→### Phase 1: Scan and review — understand the codebase
    27→
    28→```bash
    29→desloppify scan --path .       # analyse the codebase
    30→desloppify status              # check scores — are we at target?
    31→```
    32→
    33→The scan will tell you if subjective dimensions need review. Follow its instructions. To trigger a review manually:
    34→```bash
    35→desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import
    36→```
    37→
    38→### Phase 2: Plan — decide what to work on
    39→
    40→After reviews, triage stages and plan creation appear as queue items in `next`. Complete them in order:
    41→```bash
    42→desloppify next                                        # shows the next workflow step
    43→desloppify plan triage --stage observe --report "themes and root causes..."
    44→desloppify plan triage --stage reflect --report "comparison against completed work..."
    45→desloppify plan triage --stage organize --report "summary of priorities..."
    46→desloppify plan triage --complete --strategy "execution plan..."
    47→```
    48→
    49→Then shape the queue. **The plan shapes everything `next` gives you** — don't skip this step.
    50→
    51→```bash
    52→desloppify plan                          # see the full ordered queue
    53→desloppify plan move <pat> top           # reorder — what unblocks the most?
    54→desloppify plan cluster create <name>    # group related issues to batch-fix
    55→desloppify plan focus <cluster>          # scope next to one cluster
    56→desloppify plan skip <pat>              # defer — hide from next
    57→```
    58→
    59→More plan commands:
    60→```bash
    61→desloppify plan move <cluster> top       # move all cluster members at once
    62→desloppify plan move <a> <b> top         # mix clusters + findings in one move
    63→desloppify plan move <pat> before -t X   # position relative to another item/cluster
    64→desloppify plan cluster move a,b top     # move multiple clusters as one block
    65→desloppify plan done <pat>               # mark complete
    66→desloppify plan reopen <pat>             # reopen
    67→```
    68→
    69→### Phase 3: Execute — grind the queue to completion
    70→
    71→Trust the plan and execute. Don't rescan mid-queue — finish the queue first.
    72→
    73→**Branch first.** Create a dedicated branch for health work — never commit directly to main:
    74→```bash
    75→git checkout -b desloppify/code-health    # or desloppify/<focus-area>
    76→```
    77→
    78→**Set up commit tracking.** If you have a PR, link it for auto-updated descriptions:
    79→```bash
    80→desloppify config set commit_pr 42        # PR number for auto-updates
    81→```
    82→
    83→**The loop:**
    84→```
    85→1. desloppify next              ← what to fix next
    86→2. Fix the issue in code
    87→3. Resolve it (next shows you the exact command including required attestation)
    88→4. When you have a logical batch, commit:
    89→   git add <files> && git commit -m "desloppify: fix 3 deferred_import findings"
    90→5. Record the commit:
    91→   desloppify plan commit-log record      # moves findings uncommitted → committed, updates PR
    92→6. Push periodically:
    93→   git push -u origin desloppify/code-health
    94→7. Repeat until the queue is empty
    95→```
    96→
    97→Score may temporarily drop after fixes — cascade effects are normal, keep going.
    98→If `next` suggests an auto-fixer, run `desloppify autofix <fixer> --dry-run` to preview, then apply.
    99→
   100→**When the queue is clear, go back to Phase 1.** New issues will surface, cascades will have resolved, priorities will have shifted. This is the cycle.
```

1→## Comments and docs
     2→
     3→- **Keep** existing docstrings/JSDoc — only remove if clearly stale or wrong.
     4→- **Don't add** new docs to code you didn't write — unless matching an existing codebase pattern.
     5→
     6→## Claude Code Overlay
     7→
     8→Use Claude subagents for subjective scoring work. **Do not use `--runner codex`** — use Claude subagents exclusively.
     9→
    10→### Subjective review
    11→
    12→Run `desloppify review --prepare` first to generate review data, then use Claude subagents:
    13→
    14→1. **Prepare**: `desloppify review --prepare` — writes `query.json` and `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json`.
    15→2. **Launch subagents**: Split the review across N parallel Claude subagents (one message, multiple Task calls). Each agent reviews a subset of dimensions.
    16→3. **Merge & import**: Merge agent outputs, then `desloppify review --import merged.json --manual-override --attest "Claude subagents ran blind reviews against review_packet_blind.json" --scan-after-import`.
    17→
    18→#### How to split dimensions across subagents
    19→
    20→- Read `dimension_prompts` from `query.json` — it lists all dimensions with their definitions and seed files.
    21→- Read `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json` for the blind packet (no score targets, no anchoring data).
    22→- Group dimensions into 3-4 batches by theme (e.g., architecture, code quality, testing, conventions).
    23→- Launch one Task agent per batch. Each agent gets:
    24→  - The codebase path and list of dimensions to score
    25→  - The blind packet path to read
    26→  - The output JSON schema (assessments + findings)
    27→  - Instruction to score from code evidence only, not from targets
    28→- Each agent writes output to a separate file. Merge assessments (average overlapping dimension scores) and concatenate findings.
    29→- Import first, fix after — import creates tracked state entries for correlation.
    30→
    31→### Subagent rules
    32→
    33→1. Use the Task tool with `subagent_type: "general-purpose"` for review agents.
    34→2. Each agent must be context-isolated — do not pass conversation history or score targets.
    35→3. Agents must consume `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json` (not full `query.json`) to avoid score anchoring.
    36→4. Score from evidence only; do not anchor to target thresholds. When mixed, score lower.
    37→5. Return machine-readable JSON matching the format in the base skill doc.
    38→6. `findings` MUST match the schema in the blind packet's `system_prompt`. Use `"findings": []` when no defects found.
    39→7. Import is fail-closed: invalid findings abort unless `--allow-partial` is passed.
    40→
    41→<!-- desloppify-overlay: claude -->
    42→<!-- desloppify-end -->
    43→

     4→
     5→1. Prefer first-class batch runs: `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import`.
     6→2. The command writes immutable packet snapshots under `.desloppify/review_packets/holistic_packet_*.json`; use those for reproducible retries.
     7→3. Keep reviewer input scoped to the immutable packet and the source files named in each batch.
     8→4. Do not use prior chat context, score history, narrative summaries, issue labels, or target-threshold anchoring while scoring.
     9→5. Assess every dimension listed in `query.dimensions`; never drop a requested dimension. If evidence is weak/mixed, score lower and explain uncertainty in findings.
    10→6. Return machine-readable JSON only for review imports. For Claude session submit (`--external-submit`), include `session` from the generated template:

    13→{
    14→  "session": {
    15→    "id": "<session_id_from_template>",

    30→    }
    31→  ]
    32→}
    33→```
    34→

    36→8. Import is fail-closed by default: if any finding is invalid/skipped, `desloppify review --import` aborts unless `--allow-partial` is explicitly passed.
    37→9. Assessment scores are auto-applied from trusted internal run-batches imports, or via Claude cloud session imports (`desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude` then printed `--external-submit`). Legacy attested external import via `--attested-external` remains supported.
    38→10. Manual override is safety-scoped: you cannot combine it with `--allow-partial`, and provisional manual scores expire on the next `scan` unless replaced by trusted internal or attested-external imports.

1→<!-- desloppify-begin -->
     2→<!-- desloppify-skill-version: 2 -->
     3→---
     4→name: desloppify
     5→description: >
     6→  Codebase health scanner and technical debt tracker. Use when the user asks
     7→  about code quality, technical debt, dead code, large files, god classes,
     8→  duplicate functions, code smells, naming issues, import cycles, or coupling
     9→  problems. Also use when asked for a health score, what to fix next, or to
    10→  create a cleanup plan. Supports 28 languages.
    11→allowed-tools: Bash(desloppify *)
    12→---
    13→
    14→# Desloppify
    15→
    16→## 1. Your Job
    17→
    18→Improve code quality by maximising the **strict score** honestly.
    19→
    20→**The main thing you do is run `desloppify next`** — it tells you exactly what to fix and how. Fix it, resolve it, run `next` again. Keep going.
    21→
    22→Follow the scan output's **INSTRUCTIONS FOR AGENTS** — don't substitute your own analysis.
    23→
    24→## 2. The Workflow
    25→
    26→Two loops. The **outer loop** rescans periodically to measure progress.
    27→The **inner loop** is where you spend most of your time: fixing issues one by one.
    28→
    29→### Outer loop — scan and check
    30→
    31→```bash
    32→desloppify scan --path .       # analyse the codebase
    33→desloppify status              # check scores — are we at target?
    34→```
    35→If not at target, work the inner loop. Rescan periodically — especially after clearing a cluster or batch of related fixes. Issues cascade-resolve and new ones may surface.
    36→
    37→### Inner loop — fix issues
    38→
    39→Repeat until the queue is clear:
    40→
    41→```
    42→1. desloppify next              ← tells you exactly what to fix next
    43→2. Fix the issue in code
    44→3. Resolve it (next shows you the exact command including required attestation)
    45→4. desloppify next              ← get the next item; repeat
    46→```
    47→
    48→**Work the queue in order.** `next` returns items by priority — fix #1 first, then #1 again after resolving. Do NOT browse the list and cherry-pick easy items while skipping hard ones. If an item is genuinely blocked or wrong, use `plan move` to reorder or `plan skip` with a note — don't silently move on.
    49→
    50→Score may temporarily drop after fixes — cascade effects are normal, keep going.
    51→If `next` suggests an auto-fixer, run `desloppify fix <fixer> --dry-run` to preview, then apply.
    52→
    53→**To be strategic**, use `plan` to shape what `next` gives you:
    54→```bash
    55→desloppify plan                        # see the full ordered queue
    56→desloppify plan move <pat> top         # reorder — what unblocks the most?
    57→desloppify plan cluster create <name>  # group related issues to batch-fix
    58→desloppify plan focus <cluster>        # scope next to one cluster
    59→desloppify plan skip <pat>             # hide from next (requires --note for accountability)
    60→desloppify plan done <pat>             # mark complete
    61→desloppify plan reopen <pat>           # reopen
    62→```
    63→
    64→**Anti-laziness rules:**
    65→- Never skip or defer items just because they are large or hard. Either fix them or use `plan move` to explicitly reorder with justification.
    66→- `plan skip` without `--permanent` still hides items — use it only for genuinely blocked work, not to avoid effort. Always include `--reason`.
    67→- `plan skip --permanent` accepts debt (widens strict gap) — requires `--note` and `--attest`.
    68→- Do not batch-browse with `next --count N` to cherry-pick. Run `next` (single item), fix it, resolve, repeat.
    69→
    70→### Subjective reviews
    71→
    72→The scan will prompt you when a subjective review is needed — just follow its instructions.
    73→If you need to trigger one manually:
    74→```bash
    75→desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import
    76→```
    77→
    78→### Other useful commands
    79→
    80→```bash
    81→desloppify next --count 5                         # top 5 priorities
    82→desloppify next --cluster <name>                  # drill into a cluster
    83→desloppify show <pattern>                         # filter by file/detector/ID
    84→desloppify show --status open                     # all open findings
    85→desloppify plan skip --permanent "<id>" --note "reason" # accept debt (lowers strict score)
    86→desloppify scan --path . --reset-subjective       # reset subjective baseline to 0
    87→```
    88→
    89→## 3. Reference
    90→
    91→### How scoring works
    92→
    93→Overall score = **40% mechanical** + **60% subjective**.
    94→
    95→- **Mechanical (40%)**: auto-detected issues — duplication, dead code, smells, unused imports, security. Fixed by changing code and rescanning.
    96→- **Subjective (60%)**: design quality review — naming, error handling, abstractions, clarity. Starts at **0%** until reviewed. The scan will prompt you when a review is needed.
    97→- **Strict score** is the north star: wontfix items count as open. The gap between overall and strict is your wontfix debt.
    98→- **Score types**: overall (lenient), strict (wontfix counts), objective (mechanical only), verified (confirmed fixes only).
    99→
   100→### Subjective reviews in detail
```

1→"""LLM-facing reporting helpers for scan command."""

    10→from desloppify import scoring as scoring_mod

    26→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

    29→def _is_agent_environment() -> bool:
    30→    return bool(
    31→        os.environ.get("CLAUDE_CODE")
    32→        or os.environ.get("DESLOPPIFY_AGENT")
    33→        or os.environ.get("GEMINI_CLI")
    34→        or os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX_NETWORK_DISABLED")
    35→        or os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX")
    36→        or os.environ.get("CURSOR_TRACE_ID")
    37→    )
    38→
    39→
    40→def _load_scores(state: dict[str, Any]) -> state_mod.ScoreSnapshot:
    41→    """Load all four canonical scores from state."""
    42→    return state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
    43→
    44→
    45→def _print_score_lines(
    46→    *,
    47→    overall_score: float | None,
    48→    objective_score: float | None,
    49→    strict_score: float | None,
    50→    verified_score: float | None,
    51→) -> None:
    52→    lines: list[str] = []
    53→    if overall_score is not None:
    54→        lines.append(f"Overall score:   {overall_score:.1f}/100")
    55→    if objective_score is not None:
    56→        lines.append(f"Objective score: {objective_score:.1f}/100")
    57→    if strict_score is not None:
    58→        lines.append(f"Strict score:    {strict_score:.1f}/100")
    59→    if verified_score is not None:
    60→        lines.append(f"Verified score:  {verified_score:.1f}/100")
    61→    if lines:
    62→        print("\n".join(lines))
    63→    # Score legend — always shown in LLM block so agents understand the scoring model
    64→    print("Score guide:")
    65→    print("  overall  = 40% mechanical + 60% subjective (lenient — ignores wontfix)")
    66→    print("  objective = mechanical detectors only (no subjective review)")
    67→    print("  strict   = like overall, but wontfix counts against you  <-- your north star")
    68→    print("  verified = strict, but only credits scan-verified fixes")
    69→    print()
    70→
    71→
    72→def _split_dimension_scores(
    73→    state: dict[str, Any],
    74→    dim_scores: dict[str, Any],
    75→) -> tuple[list[tuple[str, dict[str, Any]]], list[tuple[str, dict[str, Any]]]]:
    76→    # Build dimension table from canonical scorecard projection.
    77→    rows = scorecard_projection_mod.scorecard_dimension_rows(
    78→        state, dim_scores=dim_scores
    79→    )
    80→    subjective_name_set = {name.lower() for name in scoring_mod.DISPLAY_NAMES.values()}
    81→    subjective_name_set.update({"elegance", "elegance (combined)"})
    82→
    83→    mechanical = [
    84→        (name, data)
    85→        for name, data in rows
    86→        if (
    87→            "subjective_assessment" not in data.get("detectors", {})
    88→            and str(name).strip().lower() not in subjective_name_set
    89→        )
    90→    ]
    91→    subjective = [
    92→        (name, data)
    93→        for name, data in rows
    94→        if (
    95→            "subjective_assessment" in data.get("detectors", {})
    96→            or str(name).strip().lower() in subjective_name_set
    97→        )
    98→    ]
    99→    return mechanical, subjective
   100→
   101→
   102→def _print_dimension_table(state: dict[str, Any], dim_scores: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   103→    mechanical, subjective = _split_dimension_scores(state, dim_scores)
   104→    if not (mechanical or subjective):
   105→        return
   106→
   107→    print("| Dimension | Health | Strict | Issues | Tier | Action |")
   108→    print("|-----------|--------|--------|--------|------|--------|")
   109→    for name, data in sorted(mechanical, key=lambda item: item[0]):
   110→        score = data.get("score", 100)
   111→        strict = data.get("strict", score)
   112→        issues = data.get("issues", 0)
   113→        tier = data.get("tier", "")
   114→        action = registry_mod.dimension_action_type(name)
   115→        print(
   116→            f"| {name} | {score:.1f}% | {strict:.1f}% | {issues} | T{tier} | {action} |"
   117→        )
   118→    if subjective:
   119→        print("| **Subjective Dimensions** | | | | | |")
   120→        for name, data in sorted(subjective, key=lambda item: item[0]):
   121→            score = data.get("score", 100)
   122→            strict = data.get("strict", score)
   123→            issues = data.get("issues", 0)
   124→            tier = data.get("tier", "")
   125→            print(
   126→                f"| {name} | {score:.1f}% | {strict:.1f}% | {issues} | T{tier} | review |"
   127→            )
   128→    print()
   129→
   130→
   131→def _print_drag_summary(dim_scores: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   132→    """Print the biggest score-drag dimensions so agents know where to focus."""
   133→    if not dim_scores:
   134→        return
   135→    try:
   136→        breakdown = scoring_mod.compute_health_breakdown(dim_scores)
   137→        entries = breakdown.get("entries", [])
   138→        drags = sorted(
   139→            [e for e in entries if isinstance(e, dict) and float(e.get("overall_drag", 0) or 0) > 0.01],
   140→            key=lambda e: -float(e.get("overall_drag", 0) or 0),
   141→        )
   142→        if drags:
   143→            print("Biggest score drags (fixing these dimensions has the most impact):")
   144→            for entry in drags[:5]:
   145→                print(
   146→                    f"  - {entry['name']}: -{float(entry['overall_drag']):.2f} pts "
   147→                    f"(score {float(entry['score']):.1f}%, "
   148→                    f"{float(entry['pool_share'])*100:.1f}% of {entry['pool']} pool)"
   149→                )
   150→            print()
   151→    except (ImportError, TypeError, ValueError, KeyError) as exc:
   152→        log_best_effort_failure(
   153→            logger,
   154→            "compute score drag summary for scan report",
   155→            exc,
   156→        )
   157→
   158→
   159→def _print_stats_summary(
   160→    state: dict[str, Any],
   161→    diff: dict[str, Any] | None,
   162→    *,
   163→    overall_score: float | None,
   164→    strict_score: float | None,
   165→) -> None:
   166→    stats = state.get("stats", {})
   167→    if not stats:
   168→        return
   169→
   170→    wontfix = stats.get("wontfix", 0)
   171→    ignored = diff.get("ignored", 0) if diff else 0
   172→    ignore_pats = diff.get("ignore_patterns", 0) if diff else 0
   173→    strict_gap = (
   174→        round((overall_score or 0) - (strict_score or 0), 1)
   175→        if overall_score and strict_score
   176→        else 0
   177→    )
   178→    print(
   179→        f"Total findings: {stats.get('total', 0)} | "
   180→        f"Open: {stats.get('open', 0)} | "
   181→        f"Fixed: {stats.get('fixed', 0)} | "
   182→        f"Wontfix: {wontfix}"
   183→    )
   184→    if wontfix or ignored or ignore_pats:
   185→        print(
   186→            f"Ignored: {ignored} (by {ignore_pats} patterns) | Strict gap: {strict_gap} pts"
   187→        )
   188→        print("Focus on strict score — wontfix and ignore inflate the lenient score.")
   189→    print()
   190→
   191→
   192→_WORKFLOW_GUIDE = build_workflow_guide(ATTEST_EXAMPLE)
   193→
   194→
   195→def _print_workflow_guide() -> None:
   196→    # Workflow guide — teach agents the full cycle
   197→    print(_WORKFLOW_GUIDE)
   198→    print()
   199→
   200→
   201→def _print_narrative_status(narrative: dict[str, Any] | None) -> None:
   202→    if not narrative:
   203→        return
   204→
   205→    headline = narrative.get("headline", "")
   206→    strategy = narrative.get("strategy") or {}
   207→    actions = narrative.get("actions", [])
   208→    if headline:
   209→        print(f"Current status: {headline}")
   210→    hint = strategy.get("hint", "")
   211→    if hint:
   212→        print(f"Strategy: {hint}")
   213→    if actions:
   214→        top = actions[0]
   215→        print(f"Top action: `{top['command']}` — {top['description']}")
   216→    print()
   217→
   218→
   219→def _detect_agent_interface() -> str | None:
   220→    """Detect the current agent interface from environment variables."""
   221→    if os.environ.get("CLAUDE_CODE"):
   222→        return "claude"
   223→    if os.environ.get("GEMINI_CLI"):
   224→        return "gemini"
   225→    if os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX_NETWORK_DISABLED") or os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX"):
   226→        return "codex"
   227→    if os.environ.get("CURSOR_TRACE_ID"):
   228→        return "cursor"
   229→    return None
   230→
   231→
   232→def _try_auto_update_skill() -> None:
   233→    """Attempt to auto-install or auto-update the skill document.
   234→
   235→    Best-effort: swallows all exceptions so a network failure or permission
   236→    error never breaks the scan.
   237→    """
   238→    install = skill_docs_mod.find_installed_skill()
   239→
   240→    if install and not install.stale:
   241→        return  # Up to date.
   242→
   243→    try:
   244→        if install:
   245→            interface = resolve_interface(install=install)
   246→        else:
   247→            interface = _detect_agent_interface()
   248→
   249→        if interface:
   250→            update_installed_skill(interface)
   251→    except (ImportError, OSError, RuntimeError, ValueError) as exc:
   252→        log_best_effort_failure(
   253→            logger,
   254→            "auto-update installed skill guidance",
   255→            exc,
   256→        )
   257→
   258→
   259→def _print_badge_hint(badge_path: Path | None) -> None:
   260→    if not (badge_path and badge_path.exists()):
   261→        return
   262→
   263→    rel_path = badge_path.name if badge_path.parent == PROJECT_ROOT else str(badge_path)
   264→    print(f"A scorecard image was saved to `{rel_path}`.")
   265→    print("Let the user know they can view it, and suggest adding it")
   266→    print(f'to their README: `<img src="{rel_path}" width="100%">`')
   267→
   268→
   269→def print_llm_summary(
   270→    state: dict[str, Any],
   271→    badge_path: Path | None,
   272→    narrative: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
   273→    diff: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
   274→) -> None:
   275→    """Print a structured summary for LLM consumption.
   276→
   277→    The LLM reads terminal output after running scans. This gives it
   278→    clear instructions on how to present the results to the end user.
   279→    Only shown when running inside an agent (CLAUDE_CODE or DESLOPPIFY_AGENT env).
   280→    """
   281→    if not _is_agent_environment():
   282→        return
   283→
   284→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {})
   285→    scores = _load_scores(state)
   286→
   287→    if _llm_summary_empty(scores, dim_scores):
   288→        return
   289→
   290→    _print_llm_header()
   291→    plan_snapshot, has_plan = _load_living_plan_snapshot()
   292→
   293→    if has_plan:
   294→        _print_living_plan_notice(plan_snapshot)
   295→
   296→    _print_score_lines(
   297→        overall_score=scores.overall,
   298→        objective_score=scores.objective,
   299→        strict_score=scores.strict,
   300→        verified_score=scores.verified,
   301→    )
   302→    _print_dimension_table(state, dim_scores)
   303→    _print_drag_summary(dim_scores)
   304→    _print_stats_summary(
   305→        state,
   306→        diff,
   307→        overall_score=scores.overall,
   308→        strict_score=scores.strict,
   309→    )
   310→    if has_plan:
   311→        print("\nFollow the living plan: `desloppify next` for your next task,")
   312→        print("`desloppify plan` to view the full queue.")
   313→    else:
   314→        _print_workflow_guide()
   315→    _print_narrative_status(narrative)
   316→    _print_badge_hint(badge_path)
   317→    print("─" * 60)
   318→
   319→
   320→def _llm_summary_empty(scores: _LoadedScores, dim_scores: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
   321→    return (
   322→        scores.overall is None
   323→        and scores.objective is None
   324→        and scores.strict is None
   325→        and scores.verified is None
   326→        and not dim_scores
   327→    )
   328→
   329→
   330→def _print_llm_header() -> None:
   331→    print("─" * 60)
   332→    print("INSTRUCTIONS FOR LLM")
   333→    print("IMPORTANT: ALWAYS present ALL scores to the user after a scan.")
   334→    print("Show overall health (lenient + strict), ALL dimension scores,")
   335→    print("AND all subjective dimension scores in a markdown table.")
   336→    print("The goal is to maximize strict scores. Never skip the scores.\n")
   337→
   338→
   339→def _load_living_plan_snapshot() -> tuple[dict[str, object], bool]:
   340→    fallback: dict[str, object] = {
   341→        "queue_order": [],
   342→        "clusters": {},
   343→        "skipped": {},
   344→        "active_cluster": None,
   345→    }
   346→    try:
   347→        loaded_plan = load_plan()
   348→    except PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS:
   349→        return fallback, False
   350→    if not isinstance(loaded_plan, dict):
   351→        loaded_plan = {}
   352→    loaded_plan.setdefault("queue_order", [])
   353→    loaded_plan.setdefault("clusters", {})
   354→    loaded_plan.setdefault("skipped", {})
   355→    loaded_plan.setdefault("active_cluster", None)
   356→
   357→    queue_order = loaded_plan.get("queue_order")
   358→    clusters = loaded_plan.get("clusters")
   359→    skipped = loaded_plan.get("skipped")
   360→    active = loaded_plan.get("active_cluster")
   361→
   362→    snapshot = {
   363→        "queue_order": queue_order if isinstance(queue_order, list) else [],
   364→        "clusters": clusters if isinstance(clusters, dict) else {},
   365→        "skipped": skipped if isinstance(skipped, dict) else {},
   366→        "active_cluster": active if isinstance(active, str) and active else None,
   367→    }
   368→    has_plan = bool(snapshot["queue_order"] or snapshot["clusters"] or snapshot["skipped"])
   369→    return snapshot, has_plan
   370→
   371→
   372→def _print_living_plan_notice(plan_snapshot: dict[str, object]) -> None:
   373→    ordered = len(plan_snapshot.get("queue_order", []))
   374→    skipped = len(plan_snapshot.get("skipped", {}))
   375→    active = plan_snapshot.get("active_cluster")
   376→    print(f"LIVING PLAN ACTIVE: {ordered} ordered, {skipped} skipped.")
   377→    if isinstance(active, str) and active:
   378→        cluster = plan_snapshot.get("clusters", {}).get(active)
   379→        finding_ids = cluster.get("finding_ids", []) if isinstance(cluster, dict) else []
   380→        remaining = len(finding_ids) if isinstance(finding_ids, list) else 0
   381→        print(f"Focused on: {active} ({remaining} items remaining).")
   382→    print("The plan is the single source of truth for work order.")
   383→    print("Use `desloppify next` which respects the plan.")
   384→    print("Use `desloppify plan` to view and update it.\n")
   385→
   386→
   387→# Backward-compatible alias for older imports/tests.
   388→_print_llm_summary = print_llm_summary
   389→
   390→
   391→def auto_update_skill() -> None:
   392→    """Auto-install or update the skill document if we detect an agent.
   393→
   394→    Called unconditionally from the scan workflow — not gated on scores.
   395→    """
   396→    if not _is_agent_environment():
   397→        return
   398→
   399→    _try_auto_update_skill()
   400→
   401→    # Single post-check: whatever happened above, is the doc current now?
   402→    install = skill_docs_mod.find_installed_skill()
   403→    if not install:
   404→        names = ", ".join(sorted(skill_docs_mod.SKILL_TARGETS))
   405→        print(
   406→            f"No skill document found. Install one for better workflow guidance: "
   407→            f"desloppify update-skill <{names}>"
   408→        )
   409→    elif install.stale:
   410→        print(
   411→            f"Skill document is outdated "
   412→            f"(v{install.version}, current v{skill_docs_mod.SKILL_VERSION}). "
   413→            f"Run: desloppify update-skill"
   414→        )
   415→
   416→
   417→__all__ = ["print_llm_summary", "_print_llm_summary", "auto_update_skill"]
   418→
```

    15→    from desloppify.app.commands.config_cmd import cmd_config

    52→    }
    53→
    54→
    55→def get_command_handlers() -> dict[str, CommandHandler]:
    56→    """Return cached command handler dict, building on first access."""
    57→    global _COMMAND_HANDLERS
    58→    if _COMMAND_HANDLERS is None:

1→## Cursor Overlay
     2→
     3→Cursor supports native subagents via `.cursor/agents/` definitions. Use them
     4→for context-isolated subjective reviews.
     5→
     6→### Parallel review
     7→
     8→Split dimensions across subagents so each reviewer scores independently.
     9→Define a reviewer agent in `.cursor/agents/desloppify-reviewer.md`:
    10→
    11→```markdown
    12→---
    13→name: desloppify-reviewer
    14→description: Scores subjective codebase quality dimensions for desloppify
    15→tools:
    16→  - read
    17→  - search
    18→---
    19→
    20→You are a code quality reviewer. You will be given a codebase path, a set of
    21→dimensions to score, and what each dimension means. Read the code, score each
    22→dimension 0-100 from evidence only, and return JSON in the required format.
    23→Do not anchor to target thresholds. When evidence is mixed, score lower and
    24→explain uncertainty.
    25→```
    26→
    27→Workflow:
    28→1. Read `dimension_prompts` from `query.json` for dimension definitions.
    29→2. Launch multiple reviewer subagents, each with a subset of dimensions.
    30→3. Each agent writes its output to a separate file.
    31→4. Merge assessments (average where dimensions overlap) and findings.
    32→5. Preferred local path (Codex runner): `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import`.
    33→6. Cursor/cloud/manual path:
    34→   - robust session flow (recommended): `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude`; use generated `claude_launch_prompt.md` + `review_result.template.json`, then run printed `desloppify review --external-submit --session-id <id> --import <file>`
    35→   - durable scored import (legacy): `desloppify review --import findings.json --attested-external --attest "I validated this review was completed without awareness of overall score and is unbiased."`
    36→   - findings-only fallback: `desloppify review --import findings.json`
    37→
    38→### Review integrity
    39→
    40→1. Do not use prior chat context, score history, or target-threshold anchoring while scoring.
    41→2. Score from evidence only; when evidence is mixed, score lower and explain uncertainty.
    42→3. Return machine-readable JSON only for review imports. For `--external-submit`, include `session` from the generated template:
    43→
    44→```json
    45→{
    46→  "session": {
    47→    "id": "<session_id_from_template>",
    48→    "token": "<session_token_from_template>"
    49→  },
    50→  "assessments": {
    51→    "naming_quality": 0,
    52→    "error_consistency": 0,
    53→    "abstraction_fit": 0,
    54→    "logic_clarity": 0,
    55→    "ai_generated_debt": 0
    56→  },
    57→  "findings": [
    58→    {
    59→      "dimension": "naming_quality",
    60→      "identifier": "short_id",
    61→      "summary": "one-line defect summary",
    62→      "related_files": ["relative/path/to/file.py"],
    63→      "evidence": ["specific code observation"],
    64→      "suggestion": "concrete fix recommendation",
    65→      "confidence": "high|medium|low"
    66→    }
    67→  ]
    68→}
    69→```
    70→4. `findings` MUST match `query.system_prompt` exactly. Use `"findings": []` only when no defects are found.
    71→5. Import is fail-closed by default: invalid/skipped findings abort `desloppify review --import` unless `--allow-partial` is explicitly passed.
    72→6. Assessment scores are auto-applied from trusted internal run-batches imports, or via Claude cloud session imports (`--external-start` + printed `--external-submit`). Legacy attested external import via `--attested-external` remains supported.
    73→
    74→<!-- desloppify-overlay: cursor -->
    75→<!-- desloppify-end -->
    76→
```

```
1→<!-- desloppify-begin -->
     2→<!-- desloppify-skill-version: 2 -->
     3→---
     4→name: desloppify
     5→description: >
     6→  Codebase health scanner and technical debt tracker. Use when the user asks
     7→  about code quality, technical debt, dead code, large files, god classes,
     8→  duplicate functions, code smells, naming issues, import cycles, or coupling
     9→  problems. Also use when asked for a health score, what to fix next, or to
    10→  create a cleanup plan. Supports 28 languages.
    11→allowed-tools: Bash(desloppify *)
    12→---
    13→
    14→# Desloppify
    15→
    16→## 1. Your Job
    17→
    18→Improve code quality by maximising the **strict score** honestly.
    19→
    20→**The main thing you do is run `desloppify next`** — it tells you exactly what to fix and how. Fix it, resolve it, run `next` again. Keep going.
    21→
    22→Follow the scan output's **INSTRUCTIONS FOR AGENTS** — don't substitute your own analysis.
    23→
    24→## 2. The Workflow
    25→
    26→Two loops. The **outer loop** rescans periodically to measure progress.
    27→The **inner loop** is where you spend most of your time: fixing issues one by one.
    28→
    29→### Outer loop — scan and check
    30→
    31→```bash
    32→desloppify scan --path .       # analyse the codebase
    33→desloppify status              # check scores — are we at target?
    34→```
    35→If not at target, work the inner loop. Rescan periodically — especially after clearing a cluster or batch of related fixes. Issues cascade-resolve and new ones may surface.
    36→
    37→### Inner loop — fix issues
    38→
    39→Repeat until the queue is clear:
    40→
    41→```
    42→1. desloppify next              ← tells you exactly what to fix next
    43→2. Fix the issue in code
    44→3. Resolve it (next shows you the exact command including required attestation)
    45→4. desloppify next              ← get the next item; repeat
    46→```
    47→
    48→**Work the queue in order.** `next` returns items by priority — fix #1 first, then #1 again after resolving. Do NOT browse the list and cherry-pick easy items while skipping hard ones. If an item is genuinely blocked or wrong, use `plan move` to reorder or `plan skip` with a note — don't silently move on.
    49→
    50→Score may temporarily drop after fixes — cascade effects are normal, keep going.
    51→If `next` suggests an auto-fixer, run `desloppify fix <fixer> --dry-run` to preview, then apply.
    52→
    53→**To be strategic**, use `plan` to shape what `next` gives you:
    54→```bash
    55→desloppify plan                        # see the full ordered queue
    56→desloppify plan move <pat> top         # reorder — what unblocks the most?
    57→desloppify plan cluster create <name>  # group related issues to batch-fix
    58→desloppify plan focus <cluster>        # scope next to one cluster
    59→desloppify plan skip <pat>             # hide from next (requires --note for accountability)
    60→desloppify plan done <pat>             # mark complete
    61→desloppify plan reopen <pat>           # reopen
    62→```
    63→
    64→**Anti-laziness rules:**
    65→- Never skip or defer items just because they are large or hard. Either fix them or use `plan move` to explicitly reorder with justification.
    66→- `plan skip` without `--permanent` still hides items — use it only for genuinely blocked work, not to avoid effort. Always include `--reason`.
    67→- `plan skip --permanent` accepts debt (widens strict gap) — requires `--note` and `--attest`.
    68→- Do not batch-browse with `next --count N` to cherry-pick. Run `next` (single item), fix it, resolve, repeat.
    69→
    70→### Subjective reviews
    71→
    72→The scan will prompt you when a subjective review is needed — just follow its instructions.
    73→If you need to trigger one manually:
    74→```bash
    75→desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import
    76→```
    77→
    78→### Other useful commands
    79→
    80→```bash
    81→desloppify next --count 5                         # top 5 priorities
    82→desloppify next --cluster <name>                  # drill into a cluster
    83→desloppify show <pattern>                         # filter by file/detector/ID
    84→desloppify show --status open                     # all open findings
    85→desloppify plan skip --permanent "<id>" --note "reason" # accept debt (lowers strict score)
    86→desloppify scan --path . --reset-subjective       # reset subjective baseline to 0
    87→```
    88→
    89→## 3. Reference
    90→
    91→### How scoring works
    92→
    93→Overall score = **40% mechanical** + **60% subjective**.
    94→
    95→- **Mechanical (40%)**: auto-detected issues — duplication, dead code, smells, unused imports, security. Fixed by changing code and rescanning.
    96→- **Subjective (60%)**: design quality review — naming, error handling, abstractions, clarity. Starts at **0%** until reviewed. The scan will prompt you when a review is needed.
    97→- **Strict score** is the north star: wontfix items count as open. The gap between overall and strict is your wontfix debt.
    98→- **Score types**: overall (lenient), strict (wontfix counts), objective (mechanical only), verified (confirmed fixes only).
    99→
   100→### Subjective reviews in detail
   101→
   102→- **Preferred**: `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import` — does everything in one command.
   103→- **Manual path**: `desloppify review --prepare` → review per dimension → `desloppify review --import file.json`.
   104→- Import first, fix after — import creates tracked state entries for correlation.
   105→- Integrity: reviewers score from evidence only. Scores hitting exact targets trigger auto-reset.
   106→- Even moderate scores (60-80) dramatically improve overall health.
   107→- Stale dimensions auto-surface in `next` — just follow the queue.
   108→
   109→### Key concepts
   110→
   111→- **Tiers**: T1 auto-fix → T2 quick manual → T3 judgment call → T4 major refactor.
   112→- **Auto-clusters**: related findings are auto-grouped in `next`. Drill in with `next --cluster <name>`.
   113→- **Zones**: production/script (scored), test/config/generated/vendor (not scored). Fix with `zone set`.
   114→- **Wontfix cost**: widens the lenient↔strict gap. Challenge past decisions when the gap grows.
   115→- Score can temporarily drop after fixes (cascade effects are normal).
   116→
   117→## 4. Escalate Tool Issues Upstream
   118→
   119→When desloppify itself appears wrong or inconsistent:
   120→
   121→1. Capture a minimal repro (`command`, `path`, `expected`, `actual`).
   122→2. Open a GitHub issue in `peteromallet/desloppify`.
   123→3. If you can fix it safely, open a PR linked to that issue.
   124→4. If unsure whether it is tool bug vs user workflow, issue first, PR second.
   125→
   126→## Prerequisite
   127→
   128→`command -v desloppify >/dev/null 2>&1 && echo "desloppify: installed" || echo "NOT INSTALLED — run: pip install --upgrade git+https://github.com/peteromallet/desloppify.git"`
   129→
   130→<!-- desloppify-end -->
   131→
   132→## Claude Code Overlay
   133→
   134→Use Claude subagents for subjective scoring work that should be context-isolated.
   135→
   136→### Subjective review
   137→
   138→1. **Preferred**: `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import` — does everything in one command.
   139→2. **Claude cloud path**: `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude` → use generated `claude_launch_prompt.md` + `review_result.template.json` → run printed `desloppify review --external-submit --session-id <id> --import <file>`.
   140→3. **Manual path**: split dimensions across N subagents (one message, multiple Task calls), merge outputs, then `desloppify review --import findings.json`.
   141→
   142→For the manual path:
   143→- Read `dimension_prompts` from `query.json` for dimension definitions.
   144→- Give each agent the codebase path, dimensions, and output format. Let agents decide what to read.
   145→- Each agent writes output to a separate file. Merge assessments (average overlaps) and findings.
   146→- Import first, fix after — import creates tracked state for correlation.
   147→
   148→### Subagent rules
   149→
   150→1. Prefer delegating review tasks to a project subagent in `.claude/agents/`.
   151→2. Set `context: fork` so prior chat context does not leak into scoring.
   152→3. For blind reviews, consume `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json` instead of full `query.json`.
   153→4. Score from evidence only; do not anchor to target thresholds. When mixed, score lower.
   154→5. Return machine-readable JSON matching the format in the base skill doc. For `--external-submit`, include `session` from the generated template.
   155→6. `findings` MUST match `query.system_prompt` exactly. Use `"findings": []` when no defects found.
   156→7. Import is fail-closed: invalid findings abort unless `--allow-partial` is passed.
   157→
   158→<!-- desloppify-overlay: claude -->
   159→<!-- desloppify-end -->
   160→
```
