## Summary: Desloppify Instruction Documents Content Analysis

I've read all the instruction documents for desloppify. Here's what I found:

### Files Present

**In `/docs/`:**
1. `SKILL.md` — base skill document (the primary shared content)
2. `CLAUDE.md` — Claude-specific overlay
3. `CURSOR.md` — Cursor-specific overlay
4. `CODEX.md` — Codex-specific overlay
5. `WINDSURF.md` — Windsurf-specific overlay
6. `GEMINI.md` — Gemini CLI-specific overlay
7. `COPILOT.md` — VS Code Copilot-specific overlay
8. `OPENCODE.md` — OpenCode-specific overlay

**In `.claude/skills/desloppify/`:**
1. `SKILL.md` — installed version (hybrid: base + Claude overlay)

---

### Section Coverage by File

| Topic | SKILL.md | CLAUDE.md | Installed .claude/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md |
|-------|----------|-----------|---------------------------------------------|
| Your Job / Core Mission | ✓ | - | ✓ (slightly simplified) |
| Workflow (Scan, Plan, Execute) | ✓ (3-phase) | - | ✓ (2-loop outer/inner) |
| Subjective Reviews | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ (expanded with cloud path) |
| Key Concepts | ✓ | - | ✓ |
| Other Commands | ✓ | - | ✓ |
| Prerequisite/Installation | ✓ | - | ✓ |
| Escalate Tool Issues | ✓ | - | ✓ |
| Runner-Specific Review Details | - | ✓ | ✓ |

---

### What's Actually Duplicated

**Near-identical content across all files (base skill structure):**
- Overall score formula: 40% mechanical + 60% subjective
- Strict score definition and importance
- [REDACTED] concepts
- Escalate tool issues section
- Installation prerequisite command

**Subjective review commands:** All runners mention both:
- `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import` (preferred local path)
- `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude` (cloud path)
- Manual path with subagents

---

### What's Unique to Each File

**`docs/SKILL.md` (Base)**
- Calls it "three phases": Scan → Plan → Execute (explicitly framed as a repeating cycle)
- Mentions commit tracking and branch workflow (`desloppify plan commit-log record`, etc.)
- Covers `desloppify exclude`, `desloppify config show`, `desloppify config set commit_tracking_enabled`
- Explicitly warns: "Don't be lazy. Do large refactors and small detailed fixes with equal energy."

**`docs/CLAUDE.md` (Claude Overlay)**
- **Very minimal** — only 2 major sections
- Specifies: "Do not use `--runner codex`" — use Claude subagents exclusively
- Describes Claude-specific subagent split workflow (Task tool with `subagent_type: "general-purpose"`)
- Emphasizes context-isolation and blind packet (`review_packet_blind.json`)
- Only 41 lines total

**`.claude/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md` (Installed Hybrid)**
- Merges base + Claude overlay into one document
- Frames workflow as **two loops** (outer: rescan, inner: fix one item at a time)
- Replaces "three phases" language with clearer "outer loop" and "inner loop" concept
- **Anti-laziness rules section** — explicit rules against cherry-picking and skipping hard work
- Adds **Claude Code Overlay** section (expanded from CLAUDE.md) with 3 review paths:
  1. Preferred: `--run-batches --runner codex`
  2. Claude cloud: `--external-start` + `--external-submit`
  3. Manual: split dimensions across N subagents
- Mentions project subagents in `.claude/agents/` and `context: fork` isolation
- Adds mention of `--external-runner claude` and session token template

**`docs/CURSOR.md`**
- Native subagents via `.cursor/agents/`
- Example agent definition (YAML)
- Three review paths: local (codex), robust session flow, durable/legacy, findings-only
- Session template JSON with required `session.id` and `session.token`

**`docs/CODEX.md`**
- "Canonical" overlay for README install
- Packet snapshots: `.desloppify/review_packets/holistic_packet_*.json`
- **Assessment auto-apply rules**: scores auto-applied from trusted internal or Claude cloud
- Manual override safety rules: can't combine with `--allow-partial`; expires on next scan
- Explicit "Assess every dimension listed in `query.dimensions`; never drop a requested dimension"
- Batch retry with `--only-batches <idxs>`

**`docs/WINDSURF.md`**
- **Windsurf cannot spawn subagents** — requires user to open multiple Cascade panes manually
- Suggests 2-3 parallel panes (user opens them, not the tool)
- Single-pane fallback for sequential review
- Three import paths (same template as others)

**`docs/GEMINI.md`**
- Experimental subagent support, but **sequential only** (not parallel)
- Agent definition: `kind: local`, `temperature: 0.2`, `max_turns: 10`
- Review one dimension group at a time, merge, then import
- Note: "When Gemini CLI adds parallel subagent execution, split dimensions across concurrent agent calls instead"

**`docs/COPILOT.md`**
- Native subagents via `.github/agents/`
- **Two agent pattern**: reviewer + orchestrator
- Copilot runs subagent calls concurrently
- Shorter than others; less prescriptive

**`docs/OPENCODE.md`**
- **Shortest overlay** (only 12 lines)
- Automatic skill loading
- Minimal guidance; mostly just points to base skill

---

### Contradictions Between Files

None. The overlays are **additive, not contradictory**. Key pattern:
- Base `SKILL.md` + overlay file = complete picture for that runner
- Base `SKILL.md` never says "use codex"; overlays add that runner's specific path

**Note:** `docs/CLAUDE.md` says "Do NOT use `--runner codex`" (general Claude principle), but `.claude/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md` says "Preferred: `--run-batches --runner codex`" (installed version acknowledges codex as option).

---

### Duplication Summary

**High duplication:**
- Scoring formula, tier/zone/cluster definitions (every overlay repeats)
- Subjective review command template (every overlay mentions `--run-batches --runner codex` + `--external-start`)
- JSON schema for review output (near-identical across all overlays)
- Review integrity rules (mostly copied across all files)

**Genuine runner-specific content (not duplicated):**
- `.cursor/agents/`, `.gemini/agents/`, `.github/agents/` paths
- Windsurf multi-pane manual workflow
- Gemini sequential vs parallel distinction
- OpenCode auto-load behavior
- Session token template details (varies slightly per runner)

---

### Architecture Observations

1. **SKILL.md is the source of truth** for base functionality; every overlay layer on top
2. **Installed version (.claude/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md) is a merge**, not a symlink — it's the production distribution
3. **Runner overlays all follow the same structure**: header tag → workflow/integrity section → footer tag (`<!-- desloppify-overlay: X -->`)
4. **Comment tags enable programmatic discovery**: `<!-- desloppify-begin -->`, `<!-- desloppify-skill-version: 2 -->`, `<!-- desloppify-overlay: claude -->`
5. **All overlays converge on the same 3-4 import paths** (local codex, external/session, manual, findings-only), suggesting these are the canonical patterns

---

### Consolidation Opportunity

If you wanted to **reduce duplication**, you could:

1. **Keep `SKILL.md` as lean base** — remove the detailed subagent instructions (they're runner-specific)
2. **Move detailed review guidance to a separate `SUBJECTIVE_REVIEWS.md`** shared by all overlays
3. **Each overlay only specifies:**
   - Its subagent spawn mechanism (`.cursor/agents/` vs `.gemini/agents/` etc.)
   - Its parallel/sequential constraint
   - Its session token template (if different)
4. **Use a build step** to assemble installed version from `{base}.md` + `{runner}-overlay.md`

Currently, the review integrity rules and JSON schema are duplicated across 8 files. A shared reference would reduce maintenance burden.

### 2. Installation & Deployment Architecture

```python

    "codex": ("AGENTS.md", "CODEX", False),                                # Shared (section)
    "cursor": (".cursor/rules/desloppify.md", "CURSOR", True),             # Dedicated

}
```

**Installation Metadata: `SkillInstall` dataclass**
```python
@dataclass
class SkillInstall:
    rel_path: str           # Where skill doc was found
    version: int            # Installed version number

```

### 3. Installation & Update Command

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/update_skill.py`**

**User Command:**
```bash
desloppify update-skill <interface>
# Interfaces: claude, codex, cursor, copilot, windsurf, gemini, opencode
# Auto-detects interface if omitted (via env vars or existing install)
```

**How it works:**

1. **Download flow** (`_download()` function):
   - Downloads from GitHub raw: `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/peteromallet/desloppify/main/docs/`
   - Downloads `SKILL.md` (base) + `{OVERLAY_NAME}.md` (e.g., `CLAUDE.md`)
   - Both are combined via `_build_section()` which:
     - Appends base + overlay content
     - Preserves version markers and overlay comments

2. **Installation strategy** (two modes):
   - **Dedicated files** (True): Completely overwrite the target file
     - `claude`, `opencode`, `cursor` → dedicated skill files
   - **Shared files** (False): Section-based replacement in existing doc
     - `codex`, `copilot`, `windsurf`, `gemini` → merged into `AGENTS.md` using markers

3. **Shared file markers** (for section replacement):
   ```html
   <!-- desloppify-begin -->
   <!-- desloppify-skill-version: 2 -->
   ... content ...
   <!-- desloppify-overlay: claude -->
   <!-- desloppify-end -->
   ```
   - First `<!-- desloppify-begin -->` and last `<!-- desloppify-end -->` are found
   - Content between is replaced entirely
   - Overlay marker preserved for later detection

4. **File path creation**:
   - `target_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` — creates directories
   - Uses `safe_write_text()` for atomic writes

### 4. Auto-Update at Runtime

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/reporting/agent_context.py`**

Called automatically during `desloppify scan` when running in agent environment:

```python
def auto_update_skill() -> None:
    """Auto-install or update the skill document if we detect an agent."""
    if not _is_agent_environment():  # Checks CLAUDE_CODE, DESLOPPIFY_AGENT, etc.
        return
    
    _try_auto_update_skill()  # Best-effort; swallows exceptions
    
    # Post-check: warn if skill is missing or stale
    install = skill_docs_mod.find_installed_skill()
    if not install:
        # Print installation instructions
    elif install.stale:
        # Print update prompt
```

**Agent detection** (via environment variables):
- `CLAUDE_CODE` → Claude Code (Claude AI)
- `GEMINI_CLI` → Google Gemini
- `CODEX_SANDBOX` or `CODEX_SANDBOX_NETWORK_DISABLED` → GitHub Codex
- `CURSOR_TRACE_ID` → Cursor IDE
- `DESLOPPIFY_AGENT` → Generic agent marker

### 5. Relationship Between Files

**Source of Truth Hierarchy:**

1. **`docs/SKILL.md`** — Single source of truth for base workflow
   - Core 1-4 sections apply to all agents
   - Version marker at top
   - Downloaded by `update_skill.py`

2. **`docs/{OVERLAY}.md`** — Interface-specific instructions
   - Each overlay is a standalone file
   - Contains only the overlay section (marked with `<!-- desloppify-overlay: name -->`)
   - Downloaded and merged with base by `update_skill.py`
   - Overlays are NOT auto-generated; they're manually maintained

3. **`.claude/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md`** — Installed documentation
   - **NOT** manually edited — regenerated by `update-skill` command
   - Contains base + overlay merged together
   - Version marker present for staleness detection
   - Read by Claude Code when scanning project

4. **`AGENTS.md`** (optional, shared files only)
   - Codex, Copilot, Windsurf, Gemini share a single `AGENTS.md`
   - Each has its own section with section markers
   - Multiple sections can coexist in one file

### 6. Version Control & Staleness Detection

**Version System:**
```python
SKILL_VERSION_RE = re.compile(r"<!--\s*desloppify-skill-version:\s*(\d+)\s*-->")

# In skill_docs.py:
SKILL_VERSION = 2  # Bump when docs/SKILL.md changes significantly

# Detection:
find_installed_skill() → SkillInstall(version=X, stale=(X < SKILL_VERSION))
check_skill_version() → Returns warning if stale
```

**How stale detection works:**
1. Scan reads version from installed skill doc's HTML comment
2. Compares to `SKILL_VERSION` constant
3. If `installed < current`, flags `stale=True`
4. On next agent run, auto-update is triggered

```python
    )
```

### 11. File Installation Locations by Interface

### Key Insights

1. **No code generation**: Skill docs are entirely manually maintained in `docs/`, never generated by code.
2. **Single source of truth**: `docs/SKILL.md` + `docs/{OVERLAY}.md` are canonical; installed files are downloaded copies.
3. **Versioning via comments**: Version is tracked in HTML comments (e.g., `<!-- desloppify-skill-version: 2 -->`), not in filenames.
4. **Two installation patterns**:
   - **Dedicated files**: Complete file overwrite (Claude, OpenCode, Cursor)
   - **Shared files**: Section-based merge into existing doc (Codex/Copilot/Windsurf/Gemini into `AGENTS.md`)
5. **Auto-update mechanism**: Runs best-effort during `scan` in agent environment; detects agent via env vars; swallows exceptions.
6. **No runner-specific scaffolding code**: The scaffold generator (`dev_scaffold_templates.py`) is only for language plugins, not agent documents.

<summary>
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   - User initially asked why desloppify removes all docstrings when agents use it to fix codebases
   - User believed there was a specific "comment" in the code causing docstring removal
   - After extensive investigation, the root cause was identified as Claude Code's system instruction behavior, not desloppify code
   - User wanted a balanced instruction added to prevent docstring removal without encouraging AI-generated comment bloat
   - User then asked to audit all skill/instruction documents across the project and create a plan to unify/deduplicate them

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Desloppify skill doc architecture**: Base `docs/SKILL.md` + 7 runner-specific overlay files (CLAUDE, CODEX, CURSOR, WINDSURF, GEMINI, COPILOT, OPENCODE) that get merged via `update-skill` command
   - **Installation system**: `desloppify update-skill <interface>` downloads base + overlay from GitHub, merges them via `_build_section()`, writes to runner-specific paths (dedicated files for Claude/Cursor/OpenCode, section-based for Codex/Copilot/Windsurf/Gemini in AGENTS.md)
   - **Version tracking**: HTML comment markers (`<!-- desloppify-skill-version: 2 -->`) enable staleness detection; auto-update runs during `scan` in agent environments
   - **Overlay markers**: `<!-- desloppify-begin -->`, `<!-- desloppify-overlay: X -->`, `<!-- desloppify-end -->` for section-based replacement in shared files
   - **Body normalization**: `normalize_py_body()` and `normalize_ts_body()` strip docstrings/comments for duplicate detection hashing only — never modify source files
   - **Comment deletion in fixers**: `_mark_orphaned_comments()` in logs.py and preceding-comment removal in useeffect.py both correctly use `.startswith("//")` — only single-line comments, not JSDoc
   - **Dual-purpose file problem**: `docs/CLAUDE.md` serves as both the Claude overlay for end users AND the project-level CLAUDE.md for desloppify development

4. Errors and fixes:
   - **False positive on `startswith("/")`**: I initially reported that `useeffect.py:38` and `logs.py:355` used `.startswith("/")` (single slash) which would catch JSDoc. The grep output truncated the display. Upon reading the actual files, both correctly use `.startswith("//")` (double slash). Had to correct myself to the user.
   - **Edit failed on useeffect.py**: Attempted to fix the supposed single-slash bug but the `Edit` tool reported "String to replace not found" because the file already had the correct `"//"`. This confirmed the false positive.
   - **Extensive wild goose chase**: Spent many rounds searching the entire codebase for docstring removal code that doesn't exist. The user was trying to lead me to the answer (it's the AI agent's behavior, influenced by system instructions) but I kept looking for code-level causes.

5. Problem Solving:
   - **Root cause of docstring removal**: Identified as Claude Code's system instruction "Don't add docstrings, comments, or type annotations to code you didn't change" being misinterpreted by agents as "don't include existing docstrings when rewriting code"
   - **Evidence from real-world diff**: The GitHub comparison `main...drjkl/desloppify` on ComfyUI_frontend showed JSDoc blocks being removed from `comfyRegistryService.ts` (function code untouched, only JSDoc deleted), `registrySearchGateway.ts` (all internal function JSDoc stripped during refactor), and `auth.ts` (JSDoc lost during code consolidation)
   - **Balanced instruction crafted**: Two-bullet instruction that preserves existing docs while preventing AI-generated bloat
   - **Doc unification problem identified**: ~30 lines of review boilerplate (integrity rules, JSON schema, import paths) duplicated across 7 overlay files
